# TPNW app (Gemini v3) — what was changed, and why

Gemini's design and features are unchanged. Only what was **needed** was changed:

1. **Facts:** every sentence on PRO and CON was checked against real sources (Oct 2026). Wrong numbers, misattributed citations and invented quotes were corrected, keeping Gemini's wording wherever it was right.
2. **Source cards:** card text now keeps only quotes confirmed word for word, with everything else marked as a summary. 32 cards were added for the sources the corrected text now cites, so you can always show the card.
3. **Works with no internet:** the original loaded its styles and fonts from the web, so with no Wi-Fi it showed up unstyled. They're now built into the file.
4. **Rebuttal siphon:** when a trigger word picks a card that only matches the general topic, the siphon now switches to the card that answers the opponent's specific claim, and it catches claims it used to miss.
5. **Crossfire:** finds the right answer far more often. When nothing matches exactly it shows the closest answer, labeled "Closest Answer — check it fits". Answer lead-ins are now grammatical.
6. **Bugs:** the speech clock reset when you opened Source Cards; "Copy Card" said Copied even when it failed; round hotkeys fired on the setup screen; the crossfire box overflowed on phones; Reset Round left the old trap filter.

## Biggest fact fixes (they repeat across the app)

| Was | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Lewis, 23 | **Lewis, 14** | "Too Close for Comfort" (Chatham House) is from April 2014. Its 13 cases run 1962–2002. Petrov broke reporting protocol; he didn't disobey a launch order. |
| ~150 US B61 bombs in Europe | **about 100** (Kristensen, 26) | 150 was the 2017–2020 estimate. Incirlik is 20–30, not 50. |
| "24–60 hours to authorize" B61 use | removed / reframed | That figure comes from a 1987 estimate (top-down 24 h, bottom-up 60 h). Release today needs a Nuclear Planning Group decision plus sign-off from the US President and the host government. |
| RAND: "5,000 killed per base at Ramstein/Aviano" | **Davis, 19**: Russia keeps escalation dominance | No source has the 5,000 figure, and Ramstein holds no US nukes. |
| NATO outspends Russia 8-to-1 "conventionally" / "adjusting for PPP" | **8-to-1 overall** (SIPRI, 26) | SIPRI totals include nuclear spending and aren't PPP-adjusted. At PPP, Russia ≈ Europe (IISS, 25). |
| European NATO "+14%" | Europe as a whole +14%; European NATO grew fastest since 1953 | The 14% figure covers the whole Europe region. |
| Russia 4,489 / 1,816 (Williams, 24) | **about 4,400 / about 1,800** (Kristensen, 26) | Those were FAS's 2023 numbers, not Williams's. |
| TPNW "legally forces" Europe to stop buying from Rosatom | a **legal and moral argument** | The treaty never bans buying civilian fuel. €781M (2025) is correct. |
| Ratifying "makes financing illegal" | Art. 1(1)(e) bans assisting "in any way", which the Ban Monitor reads to cover financing | The treaty text never says "financing". |
| 109 institutions managing $4 trillion | 109 = **PAX/ICAN, 23**; $3.9T = **PAX/ICAN, 22** | These come from two different reports. |
| "301 banks backslid 15%" | 301 financiers, up from 260 | 301 is the total number of financiers. |
| $118.8B nuclear spending (Muñoz / SIPRI) | **ICAN, 26** ("Premeditated") | Misattributed. |
| Chevreuil, 25 | **Horschig, 25** | Horschig is the lead author. The piece doesn't make the Poland/South Korea/Japan proliferation claim; Poland now cites **Tusk, 25**. |
| NAC 2020 quote | exact wording, 30 allies (then) | The 32-ally line is the 2026 statement (**North Atlantic Council, 26**). |
| 185 of 194 countries | **184 of 193** UN members | |
| 24 allies with no nukes | **23** | |
| Hill, 21 says relying on the umbrella is illegal | **Harvard Law Clinic, 18** | Hill doesn't say it; the Harvard clinic does (join → give up the umbrella, but you can stay in NATO). |

## Every fact correction (570)

Each line shows the old wording (struck out) → the new wording, why, and where to find the source.

### PRO — Constructive speech

- **Paragraph 3** — ~~Lewis, 23 from Chatham House analyzed declassified nuclear crises and found that the world has survived nuclear weapons only through sheer luck and individual officers disobeying launch protocols—including the 1983 Petr…~~ → Lewis, 14 from Chatham House reviewed declassified documents, testimonies, and interviews and concluded the world 'has, indeed, been lucky'—with individual officers, 'often in disobedience of protocol and political guidance,' saving the day in cases like the …
  - Why: 'Too Close for Comfort' (Lewis, Williams, Pelopidas, Aghlani) was published 28 April 2014; 2023 is only a web-page update. It says the world 'has, indeed, been lucky', not that luck is the 'only' reason. Petrov broke reporting protocol and was never given a launch order. The 2009 collision and the 2013 USAF misconduct are flagged in the report as recent causes for concern, so they are kept with t…
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **Paragraph 3** — ~~Kristensen, 22 at the Federation of American Scientists reports that hosting roughly 150 American B61 gravity bombs across five NATO countries paints a preemptive radioactive bullseye~~ → Kristensen, 26 at the Federation of American Scientists estimates the US still stores about 100 B61 nuclear bombs at six airbases in five NATO countries—painting a preemptive radioactive bullseye
  - Why: 150 was FAS's estimate around 2017–2020. Current FAS estimates (Nuclear Notebook, Dec 2025 and Mar 2026) are about 100 bombs at six bases in five countries. The 'bullseye' is the debater's inference, so it is separated from the count.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/
- **Paragraph 3** — ~~Sweeney, 20 at Defense Priorities proves these free-fall bombs are operationally useless relics that take 24 to 60 hours to authorize and cannot survive modern Russian air defenses, while RAND wargames show using them f…~~ → Sweeney, 20 at Defense Priorities argues these bombs are effectively useless, because the jets that would carry them cannot reliably get through Russia's air defenses in a Baltic war, while RAND's own Baltic wargames found that even modernized NATO tactical n…
  - Why: No source found gives a '24 to 60 hours to authorize' figure for NATO B61s, and no RAND study or Sweeney text found has Russian strikes 'killing 5,000 people per base at Ramstein and Aviano' (Ramstein has held no US nukes since about 2005). Sweeney argues the delivery jets can't reliably penetrate Russian air defenses; RAND RR-2781 (Davis et al., 2019) found Russia keeps escalation dominance.
  - Source: https://www.defensepriorities.org/reports/reconsidering-us-nuclear-weapons-in-europe/ · https://www.defenseone.com/ideas/2020/10/us-should-remove-its-nukes-europe/168876/ · https://shop.rand.org/products/exploring-the-role-nuclear-weapons-could-play-in-deterring-russian-threats-to-the-baltic-states
- **Paragraph 4** — ~~SIPRI, 26 reports that NATO outspends Russia conventionally over eight-to-one overall~~ → SIPRI, 26 figures show NATO outspends Russia over eight-to-one overall
  - Why: SIPRI's totals are all military spending, nuclear forces included, not 'conventional' spending, and the 8.3:1 ratio is the debaters' own math from SIPRI's numbers. (The 14 percent rise is correctly given as all-Europe spending.)
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge
- **Paragraph 4** — ~~can defeat Russian aggression by denial without ever threatening global annihilation~~ → can raise the costs of Russian aggression by denial from the very first hours of a conflict, without ever threatening global annihilation
  - Why: Van Hooft (RAND, Mar 2025) says conventional denial capabilities raise an adversary's costs early in a conflict. He does not claim NATO can 'defeat' Russia without nuclear weapons.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html
- **Paragraph 4** — ~~while European allies remain fully protected under NATO's Article 5 conventional umbrella and simply opt out of nuclear strategic planning, matching France's historical NATO posture (Hill, 21).~~ → while European allies stay in NATO's Article 5 conventional defense as long as they give up the nuclear umbrella (Harvard Law Clinic, 18) and opt out of nuclear strategic planning, matching France's posture outside NATO's Nuclear Planning Group.
  - Why: Hill's wording on TPNW/NATO compatibility and nuclear planning could not be confirmed. The real source is the Harvard Law clinic's 2018 report: a TPNW member can stay in the alliance and conventional operations only if it gives up reliance on the nuclear umbrella. France has stayed outside the NPG since it was created in 1966 (NATO).
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons · https://banmonitor.org/tpnw-prohibitions/the-prohibition-on-assisting-encouraging-or-inducing-prohibited-activities · https://nato.int/cps/en/natohq/topics_50069.htm
- **Paragraph 5** — ~~Under Article 1 of the treaty, European ratification makes financing or assisting nuclear weapons illegal under domestic law.~~ → Article 1(1)(e) of the treaty bans assisting nuclear weapons 'in any way,' which the Nuclear Weapons Ban Monitor reads to cover financing—so European ratification brings legal pressure on Europe's banks.
  - Why: The TPNW never uses the word 'financing'. Financing is an interpretation of 1(1)(e), and the treaty binds states, which need national laws to reach banks (only Ireland and Niue have TPNW-specific laws).
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf · https://banmonitor.org/tpnw-prohibitions/the-prohibition-on-assisting-encouraging-or-inducing-prohibited-activities
- **Paragraph 5** — ~~Muñoz, 26 in the Don't Bank on the Bomb report shows that 109 financial institutions managing over 4 trillion dollars already restrict nuclear weapons producers citing the TPNW.~~ → PAX and ICAN, 23 profile 109 financial institutions already restricting nuclear weapons producers, many citing the TPNW, and institutions holding 3.9 trillion dollars named the treaty as a reason (PAX/ICAN, 22).
  - Why: 109 comes from PAX/ICAN's July 2023 report 'Moving Away from Mass Destruction', which gives no assets total and says 'many', not all, cite the TPNW. The ~$3.9T of institutions naming the TPNW comes from the Jan 2022 report 'Rejecting Risk'. Neither is a 2026 Muñoz finding.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/109_investors_rejecting_nuclear_weapons_dont_bank_on_the_bomb_policies_report · https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/RejectingRisk-web.pdf
- **Paragraph 5** — ~~reveals that European Union nations still paid over 781 million euros in 2025 for uranium and nuclear fuel to Russia's state corporation Rosatom—where 38 percent of employees build Russian nuclear warheads.~~ → reveals that European Union nations still paid about 781 million euros in 2025 for enriched uranium and nuclear fuel from Russia's state corporation Rosatom—whose nuclear weapons complex employed about 38 percent of its workers as of 2016, per SIPRI data.
  - Why: RUSI gives the components (over €312m uranium, €465m fuel assemblies, €4m reactor parts); €781m is their sum. The 38% is a 2016 SIPRI figure for staff at weapons-complex enterprises, not people who 'build warheads'.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such · https://banmonitor.org/news/is-kazakhstan-assisting-russias-nuclear-weapons-programme
- **Paragraph 5** — ~~Ratifying the TPNW legally forces Europe to cut off nearly a billion euros a year flowing straight into Russia's nuclear weapons complex.~~ → Ratifying the TPNW gives Europe a powerful legal and moral argument to cut off nearly 800 million euros a year flowing to the steward of Russia's nuclear weapons complex.
  - Why: No treaty text, interpretation or state practice says the TPNW bans buying civilian nuclear fuel: the preamble protects peaceful nuclear energy, and TPNW party Kazakhstan trades with Rosatom. Also, €781m is not 'nearly a billion', and the money goes to Rosatom, not straight into the weapons complex.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such · https://banmonitor.org/news/is-kazakhstan-assisting-russias-nuclear-weapons-programme
- **Paragraph 5** — ~~PAX and ICAN, 23 profile~~ → PAX/ICAN, 23 profile
  - Why: one spelling of the same citation tag across the app

### PRO — Rebuttal cards

- **1. Deterrence Works / 80 Yrs Peace (citation)** — ~~Lewis, 23 (Chatham House)~~ → Lewis, 14 (Chatham House)
  - Why: The Chatham House report was published April 2014.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy
- **2. Weakens NPT / No Verification (citation)** — ~~Docherty, 21 (Harvard Law)~~ → Harvard Law Clinic, 19
  - Why: The NPT-compatibility point comes from the Harvard IHRC's 2019 Althingi submission, not Docherty's 2021 memoir.
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/submission-to-althingi-inquiry-into-resolution-57-149-bann-vid-kjarnorkuvopnum
- **3. Destroys NATO / Article 5 (citation)** — ~~Hill, 21 (Chatham House)~~ → Hill, 21 (Chatham House), Harvard Law Clinic, 18 & Defense News, 26
  - Why: The rewritten response rests on three sources (Hill, the Harvard Law Clinic 2018 umbrella report and Defense News 2026), but the tag lists only Hill.
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons · https://www.defensenews.com/global/europe/2026/03/09/finland-readies-itself-to-be-able-to-host-nukes-in-longtime-policy-departure/
- **4. Russian Blackmail / Monopoly (citation)** — ~~SIPRI, 26 & van Hooft, 25 (RAND)~~ → SIPRI, 26, Kristensen, 26 (FAS) & van Hooft, 25 (RAND)
  - Why: Adds the source for the ~1,700 US strategic warheads (FAS Nuclear Notebook 2026).
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **5. State Budgets / Divestment Hurts West (citation)** — ~~Muñoz, 26 (ICAN/PAX)~~ → PAX/ICAN, 23
  - Why: The 109-institution figure is from PAX/ICAN's July 2023 report.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/109_investors_rejecting_nuclear_weapons_dont_bank_on_the_bomb_policies_report
- **5. State Budgets / Divestment Hurts West (citation)** — ~~Danylyuk, 26 (RUSI) & PAX/ICAN, 23~~ → Danylyuk, 26 (RUSI), PAX/ICAN, 23 & PAX/ICAN, 22
  - Why: The response also cites the $3.9T figure from PAX/ICAN, 22 ('Rejecting Risk', Jan 2022).
  - Source: https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/RejectingRisk-web.pdf
- **6. Ukraine 1994 Disarmament Analogy (citation)** — ~~SIPRI, 26 & Hill, 21 (Chatham House)~~ → SIPRI, 26 & Kristensen, 26 (FAS)
  - Why: Nothing in this response comes from Hill. The 23-country count rests on FAS's list of B61 host countries.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **7. US Umbrella / Baltic Defense (citation)** — ~~Sweeney, 20 (Defense Priorities / RAND Wargame)~~ → Sweeney, 20 (Defense Priorities) & Davis, 19 (RAND)
  - Why: The RAND wargame finding is Davis et al., RR-2781 (2019).
  - Source: https://shop.rand.org/products/exploring-the-role-nuclear-weapons-could-play-in-deterring-russian-threats-to-the-baltic-states
- **8. Disarming France & UK (515 Warheads) (citation)** — ~~Chevreuil, 25 (CSIS) & van Hooft, 25 (RAND)~~ → Horschig, 25 (CSIS) & Tusk, 25
  - Why: Horschig is the lead author of the CSIS piece. The Poland point comes from Tusk's March 2025 speech, not van Hooft.
  - Source: https://www.csis.org/analysis/can-france-and-united-kingdom-replace-us-nuclear-umbrella
- **9. Russian PPP / European Ammo Gaps (citation)** — ~~SIPRI, 26 & Sweeney, 20 (Defense Priorities)~~ → SIPRI, 26 & Breaking Defense, 22
  - Why: The dual-capable-jet cost evidence now comes from Breaking Defense (Dec 2022).
  - Source: https://breakingdefense.com/2022/12/germany-seals-entry-to-f-35-club-with-8-8b-35-fighter-plan/
- **10. TPNW Is Purely Symbolic (citation)** — ~~Kristensen, 22, Danylyuk, 26 & Muñoz, 26~~ → Kristensen, 26, Danylyuk, 26 & PAX/ICAN, 22
  - Why: Tags updated to match the corrected sources.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **11. Polish / German Proliferation Cascade (citation)** — ~~van Hooft, 25 (RAND)~~ → Tusk, 25
  - Why: The Poland claim now cites Tusk's March 2025 Sejm speech.
  - Source: https://www.intellinews.com/poland-s-tusk-outlines-push-for-nuclear-weapons-plans-to-expand-army-to-500-000-370665
- **11. Polish / German Proliferation Cascade (citation)** — ~~Hill, 21 (Chatham House) & Tusk, 25~~ → TPNW Art. 1 & Tusk, 25
  - Why: Nothing in the rewritten response comes from Hill. Its warrants are the treaty text (Art. 1(1)(a) and (g)) and Tusk's March 2025 speech, so a Hill tag invites a call for a card that doesn't say this.
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf
- **12. Russia's 1,816 Tactical Nukes / Belarus (chipLabel)** — ~~Russia's 1,816 Tactical Nukes~~ → Russia's ~1,800 Tactical Nukes
  - Why: FAS 2026: ~1,800 non-strategic; 1,816 was the 2023 estimate
- **12. Russia's 1,816 Tactical Nukes / Belarus (trigger)** — ~~Russia's 1,816 tactical nukes~~ → Russia's roughly 1,800 tactical nukes
  - Why: 1,816 is FAS's 2023 estimate; 'Russian nuclear weapons, 2026' gives about 1,794 non-strategic.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **12. Russia's 1,816 Tactical Nukes / Belarus (citation)** — ~~Williams, 24 (CSIS) & Sweeney, 20 (Defense Priorities)~~ → Al Jazeera, 23, Shlapak, 16 (RAND) & Sweeney, 20 (Defense Priorities)
  - Why: Tags updated to match the corrected sources.
  - Source: https://www.aljazeera.com/news/2023/3/28/why-does-russia-want-tactical-nuclear-weapons-in-belarus
- **13. 1983 Petrov Is Old / Modern Locks (citation)** — ~~Lewis, 23 (Chatham House)~~ → Lewis, 14 (Chatham House) & AP, 10
  - Why: This citation was missed. The response now reads 'Lewis, 14… (AP, 10)', but the displayed tag still says Lewis, 23. 'Too Close for Comfort' was published 28 Apr 2014, and the Minuteman outage is sourced to AP (Oct 2010).
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.washingtontimes.com/news/2010/oct/27/pentagon-equipment-outage-wyo-nuke-base/
- **14. EU Can Sanction Rosatom Without TPNW (citation)** — ~~Danylyuk, 26 (RUSI) & Muñoz, 26 (ICAN/PAX)~~ → Danylyuk, 26 (RUSI), Bellona, 26 & Muñoz, 26 (ICAN/PAX)
  - Why: The response now cites Bellona, 26 for Lingen.
  - Source: https://bellona.org/news/nuclear-issues/2026-09-germany-wants-independence-from-russian-nuclear-fuel-using-rosatom-as-a-bridge
- **1. Deterrence Works / 80 Yrs Peace (response)** — ~~Lewis, 23 at Chatham House proves we survived the Cold War only by sheer luck and officers disobeying orders, while~~ → Lewis, 14 at Chatham House found the world 'has, indeed, been lucky'—with officers acting 'often in disobedience of protocol and political guidance' saving the day, not deterrence alone—while
  - Why: The report dates from 28 Apr 2014. It says the world has 'been lucky' and that individuals often disobeyed protocol; it does not say luck was the only reason, and Petrov disobeyed reporting protocol, not orders.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **1. Deterrence Works / 80 Yrs Peace (response)** — ~~Jones, 25 at CSIS testified that Russia tripled kinetic sabotage and bombing attacks inside NATO Europe between 2023 and 2024.~~ → Jones, 25 at CSIS found Russian sabotage attacks across Europe nearly tripled from 2023 to 2024.
  - Why: The source is the CSIS report 'Russia's Shadow War Against the West' (Jones, Mar 2025), not his Nov 2025 testimony. Attacks in Europe 'nearly tripled' (12 to 34).
  - Source: https://csis.org/analysis/russias-shadow-war-against-west
- **2. Weakens NPT / No Verification (response)** — ~~yet Docherty, 21 at Harvard Law confirms the TPNW directly enforces NPT Article VI's disarmament mandate.~~ → yet Harvard Law's human rights clinic (Harvard Law Clinic, 19) concludes joining the TPNW helps states better meet their NPT obligations—exactly the kind of effective disarmament measure NPT Article VI calls for.
  - Why: Docherty's 2021 piece is a memoir ('Banning Nuclear Weapons: Milestones and Memories') and doesn't make this argument. The NPT point comes from the Harvard clinic's 2019 Althingi submission. Article VI requires good-faith negotiations, not disarmament as such, and the TPNW cannot 'enforce' the NPT.
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/submission-to-althingi-inquiry-into-resolution-57-149-bann-vid-kjarnorkuvopnum · https://www.un.org/disarmament/wmd/nuclear/npt/text/
- **2. Weakens NPT / No Verification (response)** — ~~Hill, 21 at Chatham House proves TPNW Articles 3 and 4 legally lock in IAEA safeguards while every EU NATO member already enforces the strict IAEA Additional Protocol under binding Euratom law.~~ → Hill, 21 at Chatham House notes TPNW Article 3 adopts the NPT's own safeguards standard and legally locks in the IAEA Additional Protocol for every state that already has one—and every EU NATO member already has the strict Additional Protocol in force through…
  - Why: Hill says Article 3 adopts the NPT's safeguards and keeps the AP as a legal floor where it already exists. The fact that every EU member has the AP via Euratom comes from the IAEA, not Hill.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/summary · https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw · https://www.iaea.org/newscenter/pressreleases/iaea-implement-safeguards-additional-protocols-eu
- **3. Destroys NATO / Article 5 (response)** — ~~Hill, 21 at Chatham House cites the Norwegian Academy of International Law to prove the 1949 North Atlantic Treaty never mentions nuclear weapons.~~ → Hill, 21 at Chatham House—NATO's own former legal adviser—confirms the 1949 North Atlantic Treaty never mentions nuclear weapons, or any weapon at all.
  - Why: Hill says the treaty 'does not mention nuclear weapons, or any other type of weapon'. No Hill citation of the Norwegian Academy of International Law was found.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/summary · https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw
- **3. Destroys NATO / Article 5 (response)** — ~~Under Article 1(1)(e), the ban on assisting or encouraging applies only to nuclear warhead hosting and nuclear-strike planning—not to remaining protected under NATO's Article 5 conventional collective defense umbrella. …~~ → And Harvard Law's clinic (Harvard Law Clinic, 18) concludes a TPNW member can stay in NATO's Article 5 conventional collective defense, as long as it gives up the nuclear umbrella and stops assisting nuclear activities. European allies comply by ending B61 ho…
  - Why: Article 1(1)(e) is not limited to hosting and strike planning: the Harvard clinic (2018) finds a TPNW member must also give up reliance on the nuclear umbrella, though it can stay in the alliance. Hosting is barred by 1(1)(g). France has stayed out of the NPG since its 1966 creation (correct). Norway's and Denmark's are peacetime political policies, not legal bans (Defense News, Mar 2026).
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons · https://banmonitor.org/tpnw-prohibitions/the-prohibition-on-assisting-encouraging-or-inducing-prohibited-activities · https://nato.int/cps/en/natohq/topics_50069.htm
- **4. Russian Blackmail / Monopoly (response)** — ~~yet the United States retains 1,700 deployed strategic warheads outside Europe and SIPRI, 26 confirms NATO outspends Russia conventionally over eight-to-one~~ → yet the United States still deploys roughly 1,700 strategic warheads outside Europe (Kristensen, 26) and SIPRI, 26 figures show NATO outspends Russia more than eight-to-one overall
  - Why: FAS (Mar 2026) puts US deployed strategic warheads at about 1,670, so 'roughly' plus a cite. SIPRI totals are all military spending, not conventional only.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge
- **4. Russian Blackmail / Monopoly (response)** — ~~can destroy Russian military targets directly without threatening global suicide.~~ → can raise the costs of Russian aggression from the very first hours of a conflict without threatening global suicide.
  - Why: Van Hooft says conventional denial capabilities raise costs early in a conflict. He doesn't claim they can destroy Russian targets in place of nuclear weapons.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html
- **5. State Budgets / Divestment Hurts West (response)** — ~~yet European purchases directly subsidize Russia's state nuclear budget right now. Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI proves EU states paid 781 million euros in 2025 to Russia's state-owned Rosatom—where 38 percent of employees build…~~ → yet European purchases are still paying Russia's nuclear weapons steward right now. Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI shows EU states paid about 781 million euros in 2025 for Russian uranium and nuclear fuel from state-owned Rosatom—whose weapons complex employed about 38…
  - Why: EU fuel purchases pay Rosatom, a state corporation with civilian and weapons work; no source says they directly subsidize Russia's nuclear weapons budget. €781m is the sum of RUSI's Eurostat import categories. The 38% is SIPRI's 2016 share of staff at weapons-complex enterprises, not warhead builders.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such · https://banmonitor.org/news/is-kazakhstan-assisting-russias-nuclear-weapons-programme
- **5. State Budgets / Divestment Hurts West (response)** — ~~Ratifying TPNW Article 1 makes assisting Rosatom illegal under domestic law, while Muñoz, 26 shows 109 institutions managing over 4 trillion dollars already restrict nuclear contractors.~~ → Ratifying TPNW Article 1 gives Europe a legal and moral argument to cut Rosatom off, while PAX/ICAN, 23 profile 109 institutions already restricting nuclear weapons contractors.
  - Why: The TPNW doesn't make civilian fuel purchases from Rosatom illegal (peaceful-use preamble; Kazakhstan precedent). 109 comes from PAX/ICAN 2023 (no assets total); the $3.9T from PAX/ICAN 2022.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such · https://banmonitor.org/news/is-kazakhstan-assisting-russias-nuclear-weapons-programme · https://www.icanw.org/109_investors_rejecting_nuclear_weapons_dont_bank_on_the_bomb_policies_report
- **7. US Umbrella / Baltic Defense (response)** — ~~yet Sweeney, 20 at Defense Priorities and van Hooft, 25 at RAND prove the umbrella is an empty bluff because no US president would sacrifice Chicago, Toledo, or New York to save Tallinn.~~ → yet RAND's Baltic study (Shlapak, 16) warns the umbrella has a credibility problem, because it may seem highly unlikely to Moscow that the United States would trade New York for Riga.
  - Why: The 'sacrifice Chicago, Toledo, or New York to save Tallinn' line was not found in Sweeney or van Hooft (van Hooft's credibility point is about Britain and France). The real source is RAND (Shlapak & Johnson, 2016): 'it may seem highly unlikely to Moscow that the United States would be willing to exchange New York for Riga.'
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2016/04/outnumbered-outranged-and-outgunned-how-russia-defeats.html · https://www.russiamatters.org/node/19
- **7. US Umbrella / Baltic Defense (response)** — ~~Worse, Sweeney, 20 cites RAND Baltic wargames proving tactical B61 use fails to halt Russian advances and triggers Russian nuclear strikes killing 5,000 people per base at Ramstein and Aviano.~~ → Worse, RAND's own Baltic wargames found that even modernized NATO tactical nukes would leave Russia with escalation dominance—NATO, not Moscow, would have the stronger incentive to back down (Davis, 19).
  - Why: No RAND or Sweeney source found has '5,000 per base at Ramstein and Aviano', and Ramstein hasn't held US nukes since about 2005. The real RAND finding is RR-2781 (Davis et al., 2019).
  - Source: https://shop.rand.org/products/exploring-the-role-nuclear-weapons-could-play-in-deterring-russian-threats-to-the-baltic-states · https://apps.dtic.mil/sti/pdfs/AD1083960.pdf
- **8. Disarming France & UK (515 Warheads) (response)** — ~~yet Chevreuil, 25 at CSIS proves Anglo-French arsenals exist only for national survival and lack the flexible yield options and command architecture to cover Eastern Europe.~~ → yet Horschig, 25 at CSIS concludes significant strategic, doctrinal, and logistical obstacles keep the far smaller Anglo-French arsenals from covering Eastern Europe.
  - Why: The CSIS piece is by Doreen Horschig (first author) and Astrid Chevreuil (Mar 2025). It concludes that 'significant strategic, doctrinal, and logistical obstacles' stand in the way. 'Exist only for national survival' and 'flexible yield options' were not confirmed.
  - Source: https://www.csis.org/analysis/can-france-and-united-kingdom-replace-us-nuclear-umbrella
- **8. Disarming France & UK (515 Warheads) (response)** — ~~Under TPNW Article 4, Britain and France disarm only through a binding, internationally verified schedule while keeping 100 percent of their conventional forces.~~ → Under TPNW Article 4, Britain and France take warheads off operational status immediately, then eliminate them on a binding, internationally verified schedule of ten years plus up to five, while keeping 100 percent of their conventional forces.
  - Why: Article 4(2) requires immediate removal from operational status, then elimination under a time-bound plan; the 1st MSP set a 10-year deadline extendable by up to 5. 'Disarm only through a schedule' hides the immediate step that Con can read.
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf · https://www.armscontrol.org/factsheets/tpnw
- **9. Russian PPP / European Ammo Gaps (response)** — ~~Furthermore, Sweeney, 20 proves ending dual-capable B61 jet mandates frees billions of euros for conventional munitions and air defense.~~ → Furthermore, Germany alone committed about 8.3 billion euros in 2022 to 35 F-35A jets chosen to keep carrying US B61 bombs (Breaking Defense, 22)—billions that could fund conventional munitions and air defense.
  - Why: Not found in Sweeney. Citable fact: in Dec 2022 Germany approved 35 F-35As (about €8.3bn) to keep its nuclear-sharing role.
  - Source: https://breakingdefense.com/2022/12/germany-seals-entry-to-f-35-club-with-8-8b-35-fighter-plan/
- **10. TPNW Is Purely Symbolic (response)** — ~~physically removing 150 B61 bombs from five European countries (Kristensen, 22), cutting off 781 million euros a year to Russia's Rosatom warhead complex (Danylyuk, 26), and expanding 4 trillion dollars in institutional…~~ → physically removing about 100 B61 bombs from five European countries (Kristensen, 26), building legal pressure to cut off 781 million euros a year to Rosatom, steward of Russia's warhead complex (Danylyuk, 26), and expanding the TPNW-inspired exclusions of in…
  - Why: FAS's current (2026) estimate is about 100 B61s in Europe. The TPNW does not legally cut off civilian fuel purchases; RUSI calls Rosatom a key steward of Russia's nuclear weapons complex. The $3.9T figure is from PAX/ICAN's 2022 report, not Muñoz 2026, and covers exclusion policies, not 'divestment' of $4T.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/ · https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such
- **11. Polish / German Proliferation Cascade (response)** — ~~and the IAEA Additional Protocol (Hill, 21), where pursuing warheads triggers automatic EU sanctions;~~ → and the IAEA Additional Protocol, which TPNW Article 3 would lock in as a legal floor (Hill, 21), so any weapons program would breach binding EU and IAEA safeguards;
  - Why: Hill does not discuss Poland; he notes TPNW Article 3 keeps the AP as a minimum where it exists (Poland's AP is in force via Euratom, per the IAEA). No 'automatic EU sanctions' mechanism for a weapons program exists; Euratom safeguards breaches allow Commission sanctions but nothing is automatic. Poland's lack of enrichment, reprocessing and power reactors is correct (first unit due 2036).
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/summary · https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw · https://www.iaea.org/newscenter/pressreleases/iaea-implement-safeguards-additional-protocols-eu
- **11. Polish / German Proliferation Cascade (response)** — ~~whereas van Hooft, 25 at RAND shows status quo nuclear reliance is what fuels proliferation rhetoric.~~ → while Poland's own prime minister floating nuclear-related capabilities in March 2025 (Tusk, 25) suggests status quo nuclear reliance is what fuels proliferation rhetoric.
  - Why: The van Hooft commentary makes no such claim. The real source is Tusk's 7 Mar 2025 Sejm speech ('the most modern capabilities, also related to nuclear weapons'); since Con reads it the other way, 'suggests' rather than 'shows'.
  - Source: https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2025-03-07/polish-premier-tusk-to-boost-army-to-half-a-million · https://www.intellinews.com/poland-s-tusk-outlines-push-for-nuclear-weapons-plans-to-expand-army-to-500-000-370665
- **12. Russia's 1,816 Tactical Nukes / Belarus (response)** — ~~They argue Europe needs B61 bombs to counter Russia's 1,816 tactical nukes in Belarus, yet Williams, 24 at CSIS proves Putin explicitly used NATO's B61 sharing as his justification to deploy tactical nukes to Belarus in…~~ → They argue Europe needs B61 bombs to counter Russia's roughly 1,800 tactical nukes (Kristensen, 26) and its Belarus deployment, yet when Putin announced Belarus nukes in March 2023, he said 'the United States has been doing this for decades'—pointing at NATO'…
  - Why: 1,816 is FAS's 2023 estimate of Russia's whole non-strategic stockpile (now about 1,800, Kristensen, 26), not the number in Belarus. In March 2023 Putin announced the plan; deployment came later. He said 'The United States has been doing this for decades'; the CSIS (Williams) brief was not confirmed to contain this.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://fas.org/initiative/status-world-nuclear-forces/ · https://www.aljazeera.com/news/2023/3/28/why-does-russia-want-tactical-nuclear-weapons-in-belarus
- **12. Russia's 1,816 Tactical Nukes / Belarus (response)** — ~~Moreover, Sweeney, 20 proves B61 gravity bombs take up to 60 hours to authorize and cannot survive Russian air defenses, whereas NATO's conventional deep-strike missiles neutralize launch sites in minutes.~~ → Moreover, firing a B61 needs a Nuclear Planning Group decision plus US presidential and host-government sign-off, and Sweeney, 20 shows its jets can't reliably get through Russian air defenses, whereas NATO's conventional deep-strike missiles raise Russia's c…
  - Why: No source gives a '60 hours to authorize' figure (it garbles RAND's 36–60 hours for Russia to reach the Baltic capitals). NATO use does need an NPG decision plus US and host-nation approval. 'Neutralize launch sites in minutes' was not found in any source; van Hooft says conventional strike raises costs early in a conflict.
  - Source: https://www.defensepriorities.org/reports/reconsidering-us-nuclear-weapons-in-europe/ · https://www.defenseone.com/ideas/2020/10/us-should-remove-its-nukes-europe/168876/ · https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html
- **13. 1983 Petrov Is Old / Modern Locks (response)** — ~~yet Lewis, 23 at Chatham House proves near-misses continued~~ → yet Lewis, 14 at Chatham House shows the danger continued
  - Why: The Chatham House report was published April 2014. It flags the 2009 collision and the 2013 USAF misconduct as recent causes for concern, not as near-use cases (its 13 cases run 1962–2002).
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **13. 1983 Petrov Is Old / Modern Locks (response)** — ~~Modernizing networks only replaces 1980s radar glitches with 2026 cyber-spoofing inside a 10-minute decision window.~~ → Modernizing networks only replaces 1980s early-warning glitches with 2026 cyber-spoofing inside a decision window of just minutes.
  - Why: The 1983 Petrov false alarm was a satellite (Oko) error, not a radar one. The '10-minute decision window' is unsourced; 'just minutes' is defensible.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **14. EU Can Sanction Rosatom Without TPNW (response)** — ~~yet Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI proves four years of standard EU sanctions failed because governments keep granting political waivers like Germany's 2026 Lingen fuel license. Similarly, Muñoz, 26 shows voluntary ESG allowed 30…~~ → yet Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI shows Russia's nuclear sector is still largely unsanctioned after four years of war—Hungary and Slovakia block EU action—and Germany even approved Rosatom-licensed fuel production at Lingen in July 2026 (Bellona, 26). Similarly, Muñoz…
  - Why: The EU never sanctioned Rosatom, so there were no 'waivers'; RUSI says the sector 'remains largely unsanctioned'. Lingen is a Framatome/ANF plant approved 22 July 2026 to make VVER fuel under a TVEL licence. 301 is the total number of financiers (not all banks), up from 260; it is not the number that 'backslid'. Article 17 allows withdrawal and nothing enforces the treaty, so it isn't 'non-waivab…
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such · https://bellona.org/news/nuclear-issues/2026-09-germany-wants-independence-from-russian-nuclear-fuel-using-rosatom-as-a-bridge · https://www.neimagazine.com/news/germany-approves-vver-fuel-production/

### PRO — Frontline cards

- **FL 1A: 1983 Petrov Is Not Outdated (citation)** — ~~Lewis, 23 (Chatham House)~~ → Lewis, 14 (Chatham House) & AP, 10
  - Why: Same leftover as rebuttals[12]: the tag still reads 2023 while the response cites Lewis, 14 and AP, 10.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy
- **FL 1B: F-35A & Guided B61-12 Still Fail (citation)** — ~~Kristensen, 22 (FAS) & Sweeney, 20 (Defense Priorities)~~ → Kristensen, 25 (FAS), Sweeney, 20 (Defense Priorities) & Davis, 19 (RAND)
  - Why: Tags updated to match the corrected sources.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/
- **FL 1C: Removing B61s Removes Preemptive Target (citation)** — ~~Kristensen, 22 (FAS)~~ → Kristensen, 26 (FAS)
  - Why: The response now cites Kristensen, 26 for about 100 B61s at six bases. The 2022 Steadfast Noon post does not support that count (KRIS-1/KRIS-2).
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **FL 2A: $559B Surge Overcomes PPP & Ammo Gaps (contention)** — ~~Contention 2: 8.3-to-1 Conventional Superiority~~ → Contention 2: Conventional Superiority (8.3-to-1 Spending)
  - Why: SIPRI's 8.3:1 is total military spending (nuclear included), not a conventional-only ratio.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025
- **FL 2A: $559B Surge Overcomes PPP & Ammo Gaps (citation)** — ~~SIPRI, 26 & Sweeney, 20 (Defense Priorities)~~ → SIPRI, 26 & Breaking Defense, 22
  - Why: Tag updated for the F-35A cost evidence.
  - Source: https://breakingdefense.com/2022/12/germany-seals-entry-to-f-35-club-with-8-8b-35-fighter-plan/
- **FL 2B: Conventional Deep-Strike Deters Tactical Nukes (contention)** — ~~Contention 2: 8.3-to-1 Conventional Superiority~~ → Contention 2: Conventional Superiority (8.3-to-1 Spending)
  - Why: SIPRI's 8.3:1 is total military spending, not a conventional-only ratio.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025
- **FL 2B: Conventional Deep-Strike Deters Tactical Nukes (trigger)** — ~~Russia's 1,816 tactical nuclear warheads~~ → Russia's roughly 1,800 tactical nuclear warheads
  - Why: 1,816 is FAS's 2023 estimate; FAS 2026 gives about 1,794.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **FL 2B: Conventional Deep-Strike Deters Tactical Nukes (citation)** — ~~van Hooft, 25 (RAND Europe) & Sweeney, 20~~ → van Hooft, 25 (RAND Europe), Kristensen, 26 (FAS) & Davis, 19 (RAND)
  - Why: Tags updated to match the corrected sources.
  - Source: https://shop.rand.org/products/exploring-the-role-nuclear-weapons-could-play-in-deterring-russian-threats-to-the-baltic-states
- **FL 2C: NATO Article 5 ≠ 1994 Ukraine (contention)** — ~~Contention 2: 8.3-to-1 Conventional Superiority~~ → Contention 2: Conventional Superiority (8.3-to-1 Spending)
  - Why: SIPRI's 8.3:1 is total military spending, not a conventional-only ratio.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025
- **FL 2C: NATO Article 5 ≠ 1994 Ukraine (citation)** — ~~SIPRI, 26 & Hill, 21 (Chatham House)~~ → SIPRI, 26 & Kristensen, 26 (FAS)
  - Why: Nothing here comes from Hill. The 23-country count rests on FAS's list of B61 host countries.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **FL 3A: EU Sanctions Failed Without TPNW (Lingen Waiver) (contention)** — ~~Contention 3: Rosatom & $4T Financial Divestment~~ → Contention 3: Rosatom & $3.9T TPNW Exclusions
  - Why: The trillion-dollar figure is ~$3.9T held by institutions naming the TPNW as a reason to exclude producers (PAX/ICAN 2022), not $4T divested.
  - Source: https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/RejectingRisk-web.pdf
- **FL 3A: EU Sanctions Failed Without TPNW (Lingen Waiver) (chipLabel)** — ~~EU Sanctions Failed Without TPNW (Lingen Waiver)~~ → EU Never Sanctioned Rosatom (Lingen Approved)
  - Why: EU never sanctioned Rosatom, so there was no "waiver"; Lingen approval was 22 Jul 2026
- **FL 3A: EU Sanctions Failed Without TPNW (Lingen Waiver) (trigger)** — ~~Rosatom's €781M uranium trade~~ → Rosatom's €781M uranium and fuel trade
  - Why: €781m covers uranium plus fuel assemblies (RUSI/Eurostat).
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such
- **FL 3A: EU Sanctions Failed Without TPNW (Lingen Waiver) (citation)** — ~~Danylyuk, 26 (RUSI)~~ → Danylyuk, 26 (RUSI) & Bellona, 26
  - Why: The response now cites Bellona, 26 for the July 2026 Lingen approval, so the tag needs to list it.
  - Source: https://bellona.org/news/nuclear-issues/2026-09-germany-wants-independence-from-russian-nuclear-fuel-using-rosatom-as-a-bridge
- **FL 3B: 15% ESG Backsliding Proves Binding Law Needed (contention)** — ~~Contention 3: Rosatom & $4T Financial Divestment~~ → Contention 3: Rosatom & $3.9T TPNW Exclusions
  - Why: The figure is ~$3.9T held by institutions naming the TPNW (PAX/ICAN 2022), not $4T divested.
  - Source: https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/RejectingRisk-web.pdf
- **FL 3B: 15% ESG Backsliding Proves Binding Law Needed (chipLabel)** — ~~15% ESG Backsliding Proves~~ → 15% Rise in Nuclear Investors Proves
  - Why: 301 is the total number of financiers (up 15% from 260), not banks that "backslid"
- **FL 3B: 15% ESG Backsliding Proves Binding Law Needed (trigger)** — ~~301 banks (+15%)~~ → 301 financial institutions (+15%)
  - Why: The 301 include banks, pension funds, insurers and asset managers.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/investing_in_the_arms_race
- **FL 3B: 15% ESG Backsliding Proves Binding Law Needed (citation)** — ~~Muñoz, 26 (ICAN / PAX)~~ → Muñoz, 26 (ICAN / PAX) & PAX/ICAN, 23
  - Why: The response now also cites PAX/ICAN, 23 for the 109 institutions.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/109_investors_rejecting_nuclear_weapons_dont_bank_on_the_bomb_policies_report
- **FL 3C: Divestment Shifts Contractors to Conventional (contention)** — ~~Contention 3: Rosatom & $4T Financial Divestment~~ → Contention 3: Rosatom & $3.9T TPNW Exclusions
  - Why: The figure is ~$3.9T held by institutions naming the TPNW (PAX/ICAN 2022), not $4T divested.
  - Source: https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/RejectingRisk-web.pdf
- **FL 1A: 1983 Petrov Is Not Outdated (response)** — ~~yet Lewis, 23 at Chatham House documents 13 near-use crises—including the 2009 underwater collision~~ → yet Lewis, 14 at Chatham House documents 13 cases of near nuclear use between 1962 and 2002 and flags the 2009 underwater collision
  - Why: The report is from 2014 and its 13 cases cover 1962–2002, so the 2009 collision and the 2013 USAF misconduct are flagged separately as recent concerns, not among the 13.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **FL 1A: 1983 Petrov Is Not Outdated (response)** — ~~Modernizing electronics only replaces 1980s radar glitches with 2026 cyber-spoofing inside a 10-minute decision window.~~ → Modernizing electronics only replaces 1980s early-warning glitches with 2026 cyber-spoofing inside a decision window of just minutes.
  - Why: The 1983 Petrov false alarm was a satellite error, not a radar one. The '10-minute' figure is unsourced (canonical military sheet).
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **FL 1B: F-35A & Guided B61-12 Still Fail (response)** — ~~yet Kristensen, 22 at FAS and Sweeney, 20 at Defense Priorities prove B61-12 bombs still take 24 to 60 hours for political release and immediately trigger Russian retaliatory nuclear strikes killing 5,000 people per bas…~~ → yet even with roughly 100 to 120 guided B61-12s now in Europe (Kristensen, 25 at FAS), firing a single one still requires a Nuclear Planning Group decision plus sign-off from the US President and the host government, and RAND's own Baltic wargames found even …
  - Why: '24 to 60 hours' has no source found. 'Ramstein and Aviano, 5,000 per base' is not in any RAND or Sweeney text found, and Ramstein holds no nukes. FAS (Dec 2025) estimates 100–120 B61-12s. RAND RR-2781 found Russia keeps escalation dominance.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/ · https://shop.rand.org/products/exploring-the-role-nuclear-weapons-could-play-in-deterring-russian-threats-to-the-baltic-states · https://apps.dtic.mil/sti/pdfs/AD1083960.pdf
- **FL 1C: Removing B61s Removes Preemptive Target (response)** — ~~yet Kristensen, 22 at FAS proves storing 150 active US nuclear warheads at Büchel, Kleine Brogel, Volkel, Aviano, Ghedi, and Incirlik forces Moscow to put those five European countries at the top of its preemptive nucle…~~ → yet hosting about 100 US B61 bombs at Büchel, Kleine Brogel, Volkel, Aviano, Ghedi, and Incirlik (Kristensen, 26 at FAS) turns those six airbases in five countries into obvious first targets for a Russian preemptive nuclear strike.
  - Why: FAS's current estimate is about 100, not 150. No FAS text says Moscow puts these countries at the 'top of its strike list'; that is the debater's own inference, so only the count is attributed.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/
- **FL 2A: $559B Surge Overcomes PPP & Ammo Gaps (response)** — ~~Sweeney, 20 shows ending dual-capable B61 jet mandates frees billions more for conventional munitions.~~ → And Germany alone committed about 8.3 billion euros in 2022 to 35 F-35A jets chosen to keep carrying US B61 bombs (Breaking Defense, 22)—billions that could fund conventional munitions.
  - Why: Not found in Sweeney. The real figure is Germany's Dec 2022 approval of 35 F-35As (about €8.3bn) for nuclear sharing. (The SIPRI 14 percent, 559 billion, 1953 and Germany figures are correct as worded.)
  - Source: https://breakingdefense.com/2022/12/germany-seals-entry-to-f-35-club-with-8-8b-35-fighter-plan/
- **FL 2B: Conventional Deep-Strike Deters Tactical Nukes (response)** — ~~helpless against Russia's 1,816 tactical warheads, yet van Hooft, 25 at RAND Europe proves NATO's fifth-generation stealth airpower, integrated missile defense, and deep-strike precision missiles destroy Russian launch …~~ → helpless against Russia's roughly 1,800 tactical warheads (Kristensen, 26), yet van Hooft, 25 at RAND Europe explains NATO's fifth-generation stealth airpower, integrated missile defense, and deep-strike precision missiles raise the costs of Russian aggressio…
  - Why: 1,816 is FAS's 2023 figure; current FAS (May 2026) is about 1,800. Van Hooft says conventional denial raises costs early; 'destroy Russian launch sites directly' is not in his piece. US deployed strategic warheads are about 1,670 (FAS). The Ramstein/Aviano retaliation claim is not in any Sweeney or RAND text found, and Ramstein stores no nuclear weapons; replaced with RAND RR-2781.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://fas.org/initiative/status-world-nuclear-forces/ · https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html
- **FL 2C: NATO Article 5 ≠ 1994 Ukraine (response)** — ~~whereas NATO's 1.58 trillion dollar conventional umbrella (SIPRI, 26) has protected 23 non-nuclear European allies for 77 years. Furthermore, European allies legally keep NATO's Article 5 conventional umbrella while opt…~~ → whereas NATO's Article 5 and 1.58 trillion dollars in military spending (SIPRI, 26) protect the 23 European allies with no nuclear weapons on their soil, and Russia has never invaded one. Furthermore, European allies can keep NATO's Article 5 conventional umb…
  - Why: SIPRI's $1.581T is total NATO military expenditure, nuclear included, not 'conventional'. Most of the 23 joined NATO in 1999 or later (Finland 2023, Sweden 2024), so they haven't been protected 'for 77 years'. Hill's wording on compatibility is unconfirmed; the Harvard IHRC 2018 report is the real source and requires giving up the nuclear umbrella.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/
- **FL 3A: EU Sanctions Failed Without TPNW (Lingen Waiver) (response)** — ~~sanction Rosatom's 781 million euro uranium trade without the TPNW, yet Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI proves four years of standard EU sanctions failed because governments keep granting political waivers like Germany's 2026 Ling…~~ → sanction Rosatom's 781 million euro uranium and fuel trade without the TPNW, yet Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI shows Russia's nuclear sector is still largely unsanctioned after four years of war—Hungary and Slovakia block EU action—and Germany even approved Rosatom-li…
  - Why: €781m covers enriched uranium (€312m+) and fuel assemblies (€465m), not just uranium. The EU never sanctioned Rosatom, so there were no 'waivers'. Lingen is a Framatome/ANF plant approved 22 July 2026 to make VVER fuel under a TVEL licence. Article 17 allows withdrawal and nothing enforces the treaty, so it isn't 'permanent/non-waivable', and no authority says (e) bans civilian trade with Rosatom.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such · https://bellona.org/news/nuclear-issues/2026-09-germany-wants-independence-from-russian-nuclear-fuel-using-rosatom-as-a-bridge · https://www.neimagazine.com/news/germany-approves-vver-fuel-production/
- **FL 3B: 15% ESG Backsliding Proves Binding Law Needed (response)** — ~~They cite Muñoz, 26 that 301 banks—a 15 percent increase—still invest in nuclear producers, yet that 15 percent voluntary ESG backsliding is our exact warrant~~ → They cite Muñoz, 26 that 301 financial institutions—up about 15 percent from 260—still finance nuclear producers, yet that 15 percent jump, as some investors weakened voluntary exclusions, is our exact warrant
  - Why: 301 is the total number of financial institutions (banks, pension funds, insurers, asset managers) financing 25 producers, up from 260; the ~15% is that rise, not a measured 'ESG backsliding' rate.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/investing_in_the_arms_race
- **FL 3B: 15% ESG Backsliding Proves Binding Law Needed (response)** — ~~whereas TPNW ratification makes nuclear warhead financing illegal under domestic law—expanding the 109 institutions managing over 4 trillion dollars that already ban nuclear producers.~~ → whereas TPNW Article 1(1)(e) bans assisting nuclear weapons 'in any way,' which the Nuclear Weapons Ban Monitor reads to cover financing—backing the 109 institutions that already restrict nuclear producers, many citing the TPNW (PAX/ICAN, 23).
  - Why: Financing is an interpretation of 1(1)(e); domestic illegality depends on national laws (only Ireland and Niue have them). 109 comes from PAX/ICAN 2023, which gives no $4T total, and only 55 of the 109 are comprehensive bans.
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf · https://banmonitor.org/tpnw-prohibitions/the-prohibition-on-assisting-encouraging-or-inducing-prohibited-activities · https://www.icanw.org/109_investors_rejecting_nuclear_weapons_dont_bank_on_the_bomb_policies_report
- **FL 3C: Divestment Shifts Contractors to Conventional (response)** — ~~yet Muñoz, 26 explains TPNW exclusions target nuclear warhead contracts, giving BAE Systems and Leonardo a direct financial incentive to exit nuclear warhead work and focus 100 percent on Europe's booming 559 billion do…~~ → yet Muñoz, 26 lists BAE Systems and Leonardo among the 25 nuclear weapons producers that ethical investors shun, giving them a direct financial incentive to exit nuclear weapons work and focus 100 percent on European NATO's booming 559 billion dollars in mili…
  - Why: No finding says exclusion policies 'target warhead contracts'; Hall of Fame policies exclude whole companies. BAE and Leonardo work on nuclear delivery systems, not warheads. SIPRI's $559bn is total European NATO military spending, not a 'conventional market'.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/investing_in_the_arms_race · https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge
- **FL 1A: 1983 Petrov Is Not Outdated (final focus line)** — ~~Lewis, 23 proves nuclear peace survived only by luck during the 1983 Petrov glitch and the 2009 UK-French submarine collision~~ → Lewis, 14 proves nuclear peace survived by luck during the 1983 Petrov glitch and flags the 2009 UK-French submarine collision
  - Why: 'Too Close for Comfort' (Lewis, Williams, Pelopidas, Aghlani) was published by Chatham House on 28 Apr 2014; the web page was only updated in 2023, so an opponent calling the card will see 2014. It says the world 'has, indeed, been lucky', not 'only' by luck. It covers 13 cases from 1962–2002 (Petrov 1983 is one) and only flags the 2009 sub collision as a recent incident.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **FL 1A: 1983 Petrov Is Not Outdated (final focus line)** — ~~inside a 10-minute launch window.~~ → inside a launch-decision window of just minutes.
  - Why: No source gives a '10-minute' window (canon). Softened to the wording used in the corrected frontline; Lewis's 1995 Norwegian-rocket case had Yeltsin deciding within minutes.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **FL 1B: F-35A & Guided B61-12 Still Fail (final focus line)** — ~~Kristensen, 22 frontline~~ → Kristensen, 25 frontline
  - Why: The B61-12 / F-35A modernization count (100–120 B61-12s now in Europe) is from the Dec 2025 FAS Nuclear Notebook, which the corrected frontline cites as Kristensen, 25.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/
- **FL 1B: F-35A & Guided B61-12 Still Fail (final focus line)** — ~~still require 24 to 60 hours for political release and immediately trigger Russian nuclear strikes killing 5,000 people per base at Ramstein and Aviano in RAND wargames.~~ → still require a Nuclear Planning Group decision plus sign-off from the US President and the host government, and RAND's Baltic wargames found even modernized NATO tactical nukes leave Russia with escalation dominance (Davis, 19).
  - Why: No source (Sweeney or anyone) gives '24 to 60 hours' for release; it garbles RAND's 36–60 hours for Russia to reach Tallinn/Riga. No RAND study or Sweeney text has Russian strikes on Ramstein/Aviano or '5,000 per base', and Ramstein has had no US nukes since about 2005. Replaced with the documented NPG + US President + host-nation authorization chain and RAND's real finding (Davis et al., RR-2781…
  - Source: https://www.defensepriorities.org/reports/reconsidering-us-nuclear-weapons-in-europe/ · https://www.defenseone.com/ideas/2020/10/us-should-remove-its-nukes-europe/168876/ · https://shop.rand.org/products/exploring-the-role-nuclear-weapons-could-play-in-deterring-russian-threats-to-the-baltic-states
- **FL 1C: Removing B61s Removes Preemptive Target (final focus line)** — ~~Kristensen, 22 target-removal warrant: storing 150 active US B61 warheads across six European bases forces Moscow to put those five European countries at the top of its preemptive nuclear strike list;~~ → Kristensen, 26 target-removal warrant: storing about 100 US B61 bombs at six airbases in five countries turns those bases into obvious first targets for a Russian preemptive nuclear strike;
  - Why: 150 was FAS's 2017–19 estimate; FAS since ~2021 (and 'US nuclear weapons, 2026') puts it at about 100 at six bases in five countries (Kristensen, 26). No FAS text says hosting puts countries on Moscow's 'preemptive strike list'; that is the debater's inference, so only the count is attributed to Kristensen.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/
- **FL 2A: $559B Surge Overcomes PPP & Ammo Gaps (final focus line)** — ~~SIPRI, 26 proves NATO outspends~~ → SIPRI, 26 figures show NATO outspends
  - Why: The 8.3-to-1 ratio is the debaters' own math from SIPRI's $1,581bn vs $190bn, not SIPRI's wording.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge
- **FL 2A: $559B Surge Overcomes PPP & Ammo Gaps (final focus line)** — ~~and European military spending surged 14 percent with European NATO reaching~~ → and spending across all of Europe surged 14 percent, with European NATO reaching
  - Why: SIPRI's +14% is for the whole Europe region ($864bn, including Russia and Ukraine), not European NATO; made the scope explicit so it isn't read as European NATO growth.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge
- **FL 2B: Conventional Deep-Strike Deters Tactical Nukes (final focus line)** — ~~neutralize Russian tactical nuclear launch sites directly without risking global nuclear suicide.~~ → raise the costs of Russian aggression from the very first hours of a conflict without risking global nuclear suicide.
  - Why: van Hooft (RAND, Mar 2025) says conventional denial tools raise an adversary's costs early in a conflict; he never says they 'neutralize Russian tactical nuclear launch sites'.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html
- **FL 2C: NATO Article 5 ≠ 1994 Ukraine (final focus line)** — ~~a 1.58 trillion dollar conventional alliance~~ → NATO's 1.58 trillion dollars in military spending (SIPRI, 26)
  - Why: SIPRI reports total military spending, including nuclear forces; it does not separate 'conventional' spending.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge
- **FL 3A: EU Sanctions Failed Without TPNW (Lingen Waiver) (final focus line)** — ~~Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI proves four years of standard EU sanctions failed because of political waivers like Germany's 2026 Lingen plant license; only TPNW Article 1(1)(e) permanently bans the 781 million euros Europe pays …~~ → Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI shows Rosatom is still largely unsanctioned after four years of war, with Hungary and Slovakia blocking EU action and Germany even approving Rosatom-licensed fuel at Lingen; only TPNW Article 1(1)(e) gives Europe a legal and moral argumen…
  - Why: The EU never sanctioned Rosatom, so there were no 'waivers'; RUSI says the sector 'remains largely unsanctioned'. Lingen is a Framatome/ANF plant approved 22 Jul 2026 to make fuel under a TVEL licence. The TPNW does not ban buying civilian fuel (its preamble protects peaceful nuclear energy; TPNW party Kazakhstan trades with Rosatom) and is not 'permanent' (Art. 17), so this is framed as a legal …
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such · https://uatv.ua/en/the-peaceful-atom-that-pays-for-war-why-rosatom-still-escapes-eu-sanctions/ · https://bellona.org/news/nuclear-issues/2026-09-germany-wants-independence-from-russian-nuclear-fuel-using-rosatom-as-a-bridge
- **FL 3B: 15% ESG Backsliding Proves Binding Law Needed (final focus line)** — ~~the 15 percent backsliding in voluntary bank ESG pledges proves Europe must ratify the TPNW to turn nuclear divestment into binding domestic law alongside the 109 institutions managing over 4 trillion dollars that alrea…~~ → the 15 percent jump in nuclear weapons financiers, from 260 to 301, as some investors weaken voluntary pledges proves Europe must ratify the TPNW to put a binding treaty ban on assisting nuclear weapons behind divestment, backing the 109 institutions that alr…
  - Why: The 15% is the rise in financiers of nuclear weapons producers (260→301, Don't Bank on the Bomb 2026), not 15% 'backsliding' of pledges. '109' is the July 2023 PAX/ICAN report; no asset total exists for it ($3.9T is the 2022 figure for institutions naming the TPNW). Not all 109 'ban' producers (55 comprehensive, the rest partial). The treaty never says 'financing'; that is a reading of Art. 1(1)(…
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/investing_in_the_arms_race · https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/investing-in-the-arms-race/ · https://www.icanw.org/109_investors_rejecting_nuclear_weapons_dont_bank_on_the_bomb_policies_report
- **FL 3C: Divestment Shifts Contractors to Conventional (final focus line)** — ~~Muñoz, 26 shows TPNW exclusions target only nuclear warhead contracts, incentivizing BAE Systems and Leonardo to shift 100 percent of their engineering capacity into Europe's booming 559 billion dollar conventional defe…~~ → Muñoz, 26 lists BAE Systems and Leonardo among the 25 nuclear weapons producers ethical investors shun, incentivizing them to exit nuclear work and shift their engineering capacity into European NATO's booming 559 billion dollars in military spending (SIPRI, …
  - Why: No Don't Bank on the Bomb finding says exclusions 'target only warhead contracts'; Hall of Fame policies exclude whole companies. '100 percent of engineering capacity' has no source. $559bn is European NATO's total military spending (SIPRI), not a 'conventional defense market'. Matches the corrected frontline.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/investing_in_the_arms_race · https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/investing-in-the-arms-race/ · https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025

### PRO — Counter-defense cards

- **CD 1: Sabotage Proves Deterrence Fails (response)** — ~~Jones, 25 at CSIS testified Russia tripled kinetic bombings, arson, and cable attacks inside NATO Europe between 2023 and 2024 while waging~~ → Jones, 25 at CSIS found Russian sabotage attacks across Europe—bombings, arson, and cable cuts—nearly tripled from 2023 to 2024, from 12 to 34, while Russia waged
  - Why: The tripling comes from Jones's CSIS report 'Russia's Shadow War Against the West' (March 2025): attacks in Europe 'nearly tripled', 12 (2023) → 34 (2024). It is not from his Nov 2025 testimony, which went to the Helsinki Commission and was about European capability gaps.
  - Source: https://csis.org/analysis/russias-shadow-war-against-west · https://meduza.io/en/feature/2025/03/21/there-has-to-be-a-cost
- **CD 1: Sabotage Proves Deterrence Fails (response)** — ~~(Lewis, 23)~~ → (Lewis, 14)
  - Why: Chatham House's 'Too Close for Comfort' is dated 28 Apr 2014; only the web page was updated in 2023.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **CD 1: Sabotage Proves Deterrence Fails (citation)** — ~~Lewis, 23 (Chatham House)~~ → Lewis, 14 (Chatham House)
  - Why: Report published 28 Apr 2014 (Lewis, Williams, Pelopidas, Aghlani).
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **CD 1: Sabotage Proves Deterrence Fails (final focus line)** — ~~Jones, 25 proves Russian kinetic sabotage inside NATO Europe tripled between 2023 and 2024~~ → Jones, 25 proves Russian sabotage attacks across Europe nearly tripled from 2023 to 2024
  - Why: CSIS (Mar 2025) says attacks in Europe 'nearly tripled' (12→34); the count covers Europe, not only NATO territory.
  - Source: https://csis.org/analysis/russias-shadow-war-against-west · https://meduza.io/en/feature/2025/03/21/there-has-to-be-a-cost
- **CD 2: Euratom Enforces IAEA Protocol (response)** — ~~every EU ally already enforces the strict IAEA Additional Protocol under Euratom law.~~ → every EU ally already has the strict IAEA Additional Protocol in force through Euratom.
  - Why: EU members' Additional Protocols come from the IAEA–Euratom–member-state protocols (in force since 2004), not 'Euratom law'; that fact comes from the IAEA, not Hill. Wording matches the corrected rebuttal.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw · https://www.iaea.org/newscenter/pressreleases/iaea-implement-safeguards-additional-protocols-eu
- **CD 2: Euratom Enforces IAEA Protocol (citation)** — ~~Hill, 21 (Chatham House) & Docherty, 21 (Harvard Law)~~ → Hill, 21 (Chatham House)
  - Why: The response does not use Docherty, and Docherty, 21 ('Banning Nuclear Weapons: Milestones and Memories') is a memoir that says nothing about safeguards or the Additional Protocol. The corrected rebuttal dropped it too.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw · https://www.iaea.org/newscenter/pressreleases/iaea-implement-safeguards-additional-protocols-eu
- **CD 2: Euratom Enforces IAEA Protocol (final focus line)** — ~~every European NATO ally already enforces the IAEA Additional Protocol under Euratom law.~~ → every EU ally already has the IAEA Additional Protocol in force through Euratom.
  - Why: Seven European NATO allies (UK, Norway, Iceland, Türkiye, Albania, Montenegro, North Macedonia) are not in the EU or Euratom, so 'every European NATO ally … under Euratom' is wrong. The Euratom route covers EU members; matches the CD response.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw · https://www.iaea.org/newscenter/pressreleases/iaea-implement-safeguards-additional-protocols-eu
- **CD 3: Art. 5 Conventional Umbrella & France NPG Model (response)** — ~~Under Article 1(1)(e), allies remain 100 percent protected under NATO's Article 5 conventional umbrella while simply opting out of nuclear-strike planning in the Nuclear Planning Group—matching France's historical NATO …~~ → Under Article 1(1)(e), allies keep full Article 5 membership and every conventional operation as long as they give up the nuclear umbrella (Harvard Law Clinic, 18)—simply opting out of nuclear-strike planning in the Nuclear Planning Group, matching France's p…
  - Why: The only citation was Hill, 21, but Hill's text on Art. 1(1)(e) and the NPG could not be confirmed. The legal claim is supported by Harvard's clinic (June 2018): a TPNW party can stay in NATO and joint operations but must give up the nuclear umbrella. '100 percent protected under a conventional umbrella' isn't in any source; Article 5 itself doesn't split nuclear from conventional. The NPG was cr…
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons · https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/submission-to-althingi-inquiry-into-resolution-57-149-bann-vid-kjarnorkuvopnum · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_Nuclear_Planning_Group
- **CD 3: Art. 5 Conventional Umbrella & France NPG Model (citation)** — ~~Hill, 21 (Chatham House)~~ → Hill, 21 (Chatham House) & Harvard Law Clinic, 18
  - Why: Added the source that actually supports the Art. 1(1)(e)/NATO-membership claim.
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons · https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/submission-to-althingi-inquiry-into-resolution-57-149-bann-vid-kjarnorkuvopnum
- **CD 3: Art. 5 Conventional Umbrella & France NPG Model (final focus line)** — ~~so allies keep NATO's Article 5 conventional umbrella while opting out of nuclear planning just like France.~~ → and Harvard Law Clinic, 18 finds allies can stay in NATO as long as they give up the nuclear umbrella—opting out of nuclear planning just like France.
  - Why: 'So allies keep…' read as Hill's conclusion, but Hill doesn't say it (he calls an ally joining 'currently seen as unrealistic'). The finding comes from Harvard's 2018 clinic report.
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons · https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/submission-to-althingi-inquiry-into-resolution-57-149-bann-vid-kjarnorkuvopnum

  - Why: SIPRI's totals include nuclear spending; the 8.3-to-1 is an overall spending ratio, not a 'conventional' one (and the corrected rebuttal no longer says 'conventionally').
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge

  - Why: van Hooft says conventional denial raises an adversary's costs early in a conflict; he does not say it 'destroys Russian launch sites directly'. $1.58T is NATO's total military spending (SIPRI), not 'conventional deep-strike missiles'. The ~1,700 figure (FAS 2026: ~1,670 deployed strategic) now has its cite.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/
- **CD 4: US Triad & Deep-Strike Crush Blackmail (citation)** — ~~SIPRI, 26 & van Hooft, 25 (RAND Europe)~~ → SIPRI, 26, Kristensen, 26 (FAS) & van Hooft, 25 (RAND Europe)
  - Why: Added the cite for the ~1,700 US strategic warheads (FAS 'United States nuclear weapons, 2026').
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/
- **CD 4: US Triad & Deep-Strike Crush Blackmail (final focus line)** — ~~the US retains 1,700 strategic warheads outside Europe while NATO's 8.3-to-1 conventional deep-strike superiority (SIPRI, 26; van Hooft, 25) deters~~ → the US retains roughly 1,700 strategic warheads outside Europe (Kristensen, 26) while NATO's 8.3-to-1 spending edge (SIPRI, 26) and conventional deep strike (van Hooft, 25) deter
  - Why: 8.3-to-1 is a total military-spending ratio from SIPRI figures, not 'conventional deep-strike superiority', and van Hooft gives no ratio. The two claims are now attributed separately.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge · https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html

  - Why: The EU never sanctioned Rosatom (Hungary and Slovakia block it), so there are no 'waivers'. Matches the corrected frontline chip 'EU Never Sanctioned Rosatom'.

- **CD 5: Only TPNW Stops €781M Rosatom Waivers (response)** — ~~Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI proves standard EU sanctions failed for four straight years due to commercial waivers like Germany's 2026 Lingen plant license while EU states paid Rosatom 781 million euros in 2025.
