  - Source: https://humanitariandisarmament.org/2021/01/27/banning-nuclear-weapons-milestones-and-memories/
- **37. NATO Steadfast Noon Exercises (answer)** — ~~Kristensen, 22 at FAS shows that NATO's annual Steadfast Noon nuclear exercises next to Russian borders only escalate mutual paranoia and give Moscow a pretext for its own nuclear drills,~~ → Kristensen, 22 at FAS notes NATO ran its Steadfast Noon nuclear-strike drill in October 2022 in the middle of the largest conventional war in Europe since World War II, timed almost alongside Russia's own strategic nuclear exercise—exactly the mirror-image si…
  - Why: MISC-8: Steadfast Noon 2022 was centered on Kleine Brogel, Belgium, not 'next to Russian borders' (2026 was hosted by Italy). The 'only escalate paranoia / pretext' claim is not in Kristensen, who notes the timing during the war and alongside a Russian strategic exercise.
  - Source: https://fas.org/publication/steadfast-noon-exercise-and-nuclear-modernization/ · https://www.nato.int/en/news-and-events/articles/news/2026/10/08/nato-holds-annual-nuclear-exercise-steadfast-noon
- **38. New START Expiration (Feb 2026) (answer)** — ~~the 781 million euros a year Europe pays to Russia's Rosatom warhead complex (Danylyuk, 26)~~ → the roughly 781 million euros a year Europe pays Rosatom, a key steward of Russia's warhead complex (Danylyuk, 26)
  - Why: RUSI-781M: €781m is the sum of 2025 Eurostat categories. Rosatom is a civilian-and-military state corporation that RUSI calls a key steward of the weapons complex; it is not itself a 'warhead complex'.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such
- **40. F-35A Stealth Jets & B61-12 (answer)** — ~~Kristensen, 22 at FAS and Sweeney, 20 explain that putting a guided tail-kit on a B61-12 bomb mounted on a short-range F-35A still requires 24 to 60 hours for political release and still triggers immediate Russian retal…~~ → Putting a guided tail-kit on a B61-12 bomb mounted on a short-range F-35A still requires sign-off from NATO's Nuclear Planning Group, the US President, and the host government, and RAND's Baltic wargames found even modernized NATO tactical nukes would leave R…
  - Why: SWEE-2/3: '24 to 60 hours' and 'immediate Russian strikes on airbases in RAND wargames' are unsourced. RAND RR-2781 (Davis 2019) found Russian escalation dominance even with modernized NATO non-strategic nukes.
  - Source: https://shop.rand.org/products/exploring-the-role-nuclear-weapons-could-play-in-deterring-russian-threats-to-the-baltic-states · https://nationalinterest.org/node/189035
- **42. Defenseless / No Defense (answer)** — ~~SIPRI, 26 proves NATO outspends Russia conventionally over eight-to-one—~~ → SIPRI, 26 shows NATO outspends Russia more than eight-to-one overall—
  - Why: SIPRI-2: SIPRI reports total military spending, including nuclear forces, not conventional spending.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025
- **42. Defenseless / No Defense (answer)** — ~~As van Hooft, 25 at RAND Europe explains, NATO's fifth-generation stealth airpower, integrated missile defense, and deep-strike conventional missiles can defeat any Russian attack without threatening global suicide.~~ → As van Hooft, 25 at RAND Europe explains, conventional tools—long-range precision strike, fifth-generation airpower, and integrated air and missile defense—can raise the costs of Russian aggression from the very first hours of a conflict, without threatening …
  - Why: VH-2: van Hooft does not claim conventional forces 'can defeat any Russian attack'. He says they raise an adversary's costs early in a conflict.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html
- **43. Why Disarm First / Unilaterally? (answer)** — ~~removes 150 obsolete B61 gravity bombs that Sweeney, 20 proves cannot survive Russian air defenses anyway, while legally forcing European states to cut off the 781 million euros a year they pay to Russia's Rosatom warhe…~~ → removes about 100 B61 gravity bombs that Sweeney, 20 argues are effectively useless because the jets carrying them cannot reliably get through Russian air defenses, while giving European states a legal and moral argument to cut off the roughly 781 million eur…
  - Why: KRIS-1: the count is about 100, and the bombs are now modernized B61-12s, not 'obsolete'. SWEE-1: Sweeney's point is about the delivery jets, not the bombs 'surviving' air defenses. ART1E-ROSATOM: the TPNW does not 'legally force' an end to civilian fuel purchases.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://www.defenseone.com/ideas/2020/10/us-should-remove-its-nukes-europe/168876/ · https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf
- **44. What If Russia Attacks Anyway? (answer)** — ~~it relies on NATO's 1.58 trillion dollar conventional military (SIPRI, 26) crushing any Russian invasion, while TPNW Article 1 legally starves Russia's nuclear complex of the 781 million euros a year Europe currently pa…~~ → it relies on NATO's military, backed by 1.58 trillion dollars in spending (SIPRI, 26), crushing any Russian invasion, while TPNW Article 1 gives Europe a legal and moral argument to cut off the roughly 781 million euros a year it currently pays to Rosatom (Da…
  - Why: SIPRI-2: SIPRI's total is not 'conventional'. ART1E-ROSATOM: Article 1 does not legally bar civilian fuel purchases from Rosatom.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf
- **45. No Nukes Used Since 1945 (answer)** — ~~Lewis, 23 at Chatham House analyzed declassified nuclear crises and proved the only reason a nuclear weapon has not detonated since 1945 is sheer luck—including Soviet officer Stanislav Petrov disobeying launch orders d…~~ → Lewis, 14 at Chatham House reviewed declassified documents, testimonies, and interviews on 13 near-use cases and concluded the world “has, indeed, been lucky”—including Soviet duty officer Stanislav Petrov breaking protocol by refusing to report a 1983 satell…
  - Why: LEW-1/2/3/4: the report is dated 2014 and covers 13 cases from 1962 to 2002. It says 'lucky', not 'only reason… sheer luck'. Petrov received no launch order; he declined to report a satellite false alarm up the chain.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Soviet_nuclear_false_alarm_incident
- **3. US Umbrella / Baltics (answer)** — ~~Sweeney, 20 at Defense Priorities and van Hooft, 25 at RAND prove the US nuclear umbrella over Eastern Europe is already an empty bluff because no American president would sacrifice Chicago, Toledo, or New York to save …~~ → RAND's Baltic study (Shlapak, 16) warns the US nuclear umbrella over Eastern Europe has a credibility problem, because it may seem highly unlikely to Moscow that the United States would trade New York for Riga.
  - Why: Misattributed and overstated. The New York/Baltic credibility line is RAND's (Shlapak & Johnson 2016), not Sweeney's or van Hooft's, and no source found has 'Chicago, Toledo'. RAND says credibility is undermined, not that the umbrella is an 'empty bluff'.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2016/04/outnumbered-outranged-and-outgunned-how-russia-defeats.html · https://www.russiamatters.org/node/19
- **4. Disarming France & UK (515) (extraClauses)** — ~~while van Hooft, 25 at RAND notes status quo nuclear reliance is what fuels proliferation demands.~~ → while Poland's own prime minister floating nuclear-related capabilities in March 2025 (Tusk, 25) suggests status quo nuclear reliance is what fuels proliferation demands.
  - Why: VH-3: this claim is not in van Hooft. The real, citable evidence is Tusk's March 7, 2025 Sejm speech; translations vary, so it is paraphrased. The Poland enrichment/Euratom facts are correct.
  - Source: https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2025-03-07/polish-premier-tusk-to-boost-army-to-half-a-million · https://www.intellinews.com/poland-s-tusk-outlines-push-for-nuclear-weapons-plans-to-expand-army-to-500-000-370665
- **5. Breaks NATO / 1949 Treaty (answer)** — ~~Hill, 21 at Chatham House cites the Norwegian Academy of International Law to prove the 1949 North Atlantic Treaty never mentions nuclear weapons. Under TPNW Article 1(1)(e), European allies remain 100 percent protected…~~ → Hill, 21 at Chatham House—NATO's own former legal adviser—confirms the 1949 North Atlantic Treaty never mentions nuclear weapons. And Harvard Law's clinic (Harvard Law Clinic, 18) concludes TPNW members keep NATO's Article 5 conventional collective defense as…
  - Why: HILL-NAT-NO-NUCLEAR: Hill says the treaty 'does not mention nuclear weapons, or any other type of weapon'; his citing of NAIL is unconfirmed. Article 1(1)(e) compatibility comes from the Harvard IHRC 2018 report, which requires giving up reliance on the nuclear umbrella.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/summary · https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw · https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons
- **5. Breaks NATO / 1949 Treaty (answer)** — ~~and Norway and Denmark's peacetime nuclear bans.~~ → and Norway and Denmark's peacetime no-nukes policies.
  - Why: Norway's and Denmark's are peacetime political policies from 1957, not legal bans (Defense News, Mar 2026).
  - Source: https://www.defensenews.com/global/europe/2026/03/
- **6. Nuclear Planning Group (NPG) (answer)** — ~~Hill, 21 at Chatham House explains that TPNW Article 1(1)(e) only prohibits assisting in nuclear warhead hosting and nuclear-strike planning—not remaining protected under NATO's Article 5 conventional umbrella. European…~~ → Harvard Law's clinic (Harvard Law Clinic, 18) concludes a TPNW member can stay protected under NATO's Article 5 conventional umbrella, as long as it gives up the nuclear umbrella and stops assisting nuclear activities under Article 1(1)(e). European allies co…
  - Why: ART1E-UMBRELLA-NPG: Hill's wording is unconfirmed; the Harvard IHRC 2018 report is the real source, and it also requires giving up the nuclear umbrella, so (e) is not limited to hosting and planning. Hosting is barred by Art. 1(1)(g). France has stayed outside the NPG since its 1966 creation.
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons · https://banmonitor.org/tpnw-prohibitions/the-prohibition-on-assisting-encouraging-or-inducing-prohibited-activities · https://nato.int/cps/en/natohq/topics_50069.htm
- **10. 1983 Petrov Is Outdated (answer)** — ~~Lewis, 23 at Chatham House~~ → Lewis, 14 at Chatham House
  - Why: LEW-1: 'Too Close for Comfort' was published 28 April 2014; 2023 is only a web-page update. The 2009 collision and the 2013 USAF misconduct are both flagged in that report.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **14. PPP / Munitions Shortages (answer)** — ~~Even adjusting for purchasing power, SIPRI, 26 shows~~ → Even if purchasing power narrows the gap, SIPRI, 26 shows
  - Why: SIPRI-3: SIPRI figures use market exchange rates and are not PPP-adjusted. The IISS PPP estimate (IISS, 25) has Russia slightly ahead of all of Europe for 2024.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge · https://breakingdefense.com/2025/02/russia-overtakes-all-of-europe-on-defense-spending-in-key-metric-iiss-military-balance/
- **14. PPP / Munitions Shortages (answer)** — ~~Furthermore, Sweeney, 20 points out that ending expensive nuclear-sharing aircraft commitments frees up billions of euros for European conventional ammunition and air defense.~~ → Furthermore, Germany alone committed about 8.3 billion euros in 2022 to 35 F-35A jets chosen to keep carrying US B61 bombs (Breaking Defense, 22)—billions that could instead fund European conventional ammunition and air defense.
  - Why: SWEE-5: this was not found in Sweeney. Replaced with the citable German F-35A purchase approved Dec 2022.
  - Source: https://breakingdefense.com/2022/12/germany-seals-entry-to-f-35-club-with-8-8b-35-fighter-plan/
- **20. Polish Proliferation Risk (answer)** — ~~and the IAEA Additional Protocol (Hill, 21), plus TPNW Article 1(1)(a) under this resolution—meaning a weapons program would trigger automatic EU sanctions. In fact, van Hooft, 25 at RAND shows status quo nuclear relian…~~ → and the IAEA Additional Protocol, which TPNW Article 3 would lock in (Hill, 21), plus TPNW Article 1(1)(a) under this resolution—meaning a weapons program would breach binding EU and treaty law. In fact, Poland's own prime minister floating nuclear-related ca…
  - Why: Hill does not discuss Poland; he notes Article 3 keeps the AP where it exists (Poland's AP is in force via Euratom). No 'automatic EU sanctions' mechanism exists. The van Hooft commentary has no Poland-proliferation claim; the real source is Tusk's 7 Mar 2025 Sejm speech. Poland's lack of enrichment, reprocessing and power reactors is correct (first unit due 2036).
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/summary · https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw · https://www.iaea.org/newscenter/pressreleases/iaea-implement-safeguards-additional-protocols-eu
- **33. Türkiye / Incirlik Airbase Risk (answer)** — ~~Kristensen, 22 at FAS and Sweeney, 20 document that storing roughly 20 to 50 American B61 nuclear gravity bombs at Incirlik Air Base in Türkiye—where~~ → Kristensen, 26 at FAS estimates the US still keeps 20 to 30 B61 nuclear gravity bombs at Incirlik Air Base in Türkiye, and storing them there—where
  - Why: KRIS-3: 50 was the 2019 estimate; current FAS/Bulletin per-base estimates for Incirlik are 20 to 30 (Dec 2025, Mar 2026). The Sweeney attribution for Incirlik wasn't confirmed. Türkiye's 2019 removal from the F-35 program over the S-400 is correct, and it had not been readmitted as of the latest reports.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/ · https://fas.org/publication/nukes-out-of-turkey/
- **39. UK & French Job Losses (answer)** — ~~require decades of high-skilled engineering work~~ → can take up to 15 years of high-skilled engineering work
  - Why: ART4: the 1st MSP elimination deadline is 10 years plus up to 5, not 'decades'. (The 14 percent rise is correctly given as all-Europe spending.)
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf · https://www.armscontrol.org/factsheets/tpnw
- **41. Every Other Country Has Nukes (answer)** — ~~when NATO outspends Russia conventionally over eight-to-one at 1.58 trillion dollars, as SIPRI, 26 confirms, and the United States still retains 1,700 strategic warheads outside Europe.~~ → when NATO outspends Russia more than eight-to-one overall at 1.58 trillion dollars, as SIPRI, 26 confirms, and the United States still deploys roughly 1,700 strategic warheads outside Europe (Kristensen, 26).
  - Why: SIPRI-2: SIPRI's totals are not 'conventional'. US-1700: about 1,670 deployed strategic warheads per FAS 2026, so 'roughly' plus a cite. (184 of 193 and 23 are correct.)
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/global-military-spending-rise-continues-european-and-asian-expenditures-surge · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/

### PRO — Crossfire questions to ask

- **Question 4 (question)** — ~~why has NATO spent 70 years desperately trying~~ → why has NATO spent more than half a century desperately trying
  - Why: The NPT opened for signature in 1968, 58 years ago, and NATO was founded in 1949, 77 years ago; '70 years' of NATO non-proliferation is loose. Canonical MISC-3 fix.
  - Source: https://www.armscontrol.org/factsheets/ukraine-nuclear-weapons
- **Question 5 (question)** — ~~why did Russia triple sabotage attacks inside NATO Europe?~~ → why did Russian sabotage attacks across Europe nearly triple from 2023 to 2024?
  - Why: CSIS (Jones, Mar 2025) says attacks 'nearly tripled' from 2023 to 2024 (12 to 34), across Europe. Canonical JON-2.
  - Source: https://csis.org/analysis/russias-shadow-war-against-west
- **Question 6 (question)** — ~~Since NATO outspends Russia conventionally over eight-to-one~~ → Since NATO outspends Russia more than eight-to-one overall
  - Why: SIPRI figures are total military spending, including nuclear forces, not 'conventional' spending. Canonical SIPRI-2.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025
- **Question 7 (question)** — ~~keep paying nearly a billion euros a year for nuclear fuel to Rosatom, the Russian state agency where 38 percent of employees build nuclear warheads?~~ → keep paying nearly 800 million euros a year for nuclear fuel to Rosatom, the Russian state corporation whose nuclear weapons complex employed about 38 percent of its workers?
  - Why: About €781M is not 'nearly a billion'. The 38% is SIPRI's 2016 figure for staff at weapons-complex enterprises, cited by RUSI, not people who 'build warheads'. Canonical RUSI-781M and RUSI-38PCT.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such
- **Question 9 (question)** — ~~Since Article VI of the NPT legally requires nations to disarm, how does actually fulfilling that requirement through the TPNW weaken the treaty?~~ → Since Article VI of the NPT legally requires nations to negotiate nuclear disarmament in good faith, how does negotiating and joining a disarmament treaty like the TPNW weaken the NPT?
  - Why: NPT Article VI requires good-faith negotiations on nuclear disarmament, not disarmament itself. Canonical NPT-ARTVI.
  - Source: https://www.un.org/en/conf/npt/2015/pdf/text%20of%20the%20treaty.pdf · https://www.icj-cij.org/case/95
- **Question 11 (question)** — ~~already enforces the IAEA Additional Protocol under Euratom law and TPNW Article 3 locks it in~~ → already has the IAEA Additional Protocol in force through Euratom and TPNW Article 3 locks it in as a legal minimum
  - Why: EU members' Additional Protocols come from the IAEA–Euratom protocol (in force 2004), not 'Euratom law'. Article 3 requires states to maintain existing safeguards as a minimum. Canonical ART3.
  - Source: https://www.iaea.org/newscenter/pressreleases/iaea-implement-safeguards-additional-protocols-eu · https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw
- **Question 14 (question)** — ~~why have 109 major financial institutions managing over 4 trillion dollars already restricted nuclear contractors because of it?~~ → why have 109 financial institutions restricted nuclear weapons producers—with institutions holding 3.9 trillion dollars naming the TPNW as their reason?
  - Why: This merges two reports. The 109 institutions are from PAX/ICAN 2023, and only 'many' of them cite the TPNW. The $3.9T for institutions naming the treaty is from the 2022 report. Canonical DBOTB-109-4T fix.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/109_investors_rejecting_nuclear_weapons_dont_bank_on_the_bomb_policies_report · https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/RejectingRisk-web.pdf
- **Question 16 (question)** — ~~a 1.58 trillion dollar conventional alliance when it gave up Soviet warheads in 1994?~~ → a 1.58 trillion dollar military alliance when it agreed in 1994 to give up the Soviet warheads on its soil?
  - Why: The $1.58T is total military spending, not conventional (SIPRI-2). Ukraine agreed in 1994 (Budapest Memorandum), and the last warhead left in 1996 (MISC-1).
  - Source: https://www.armscontrol.org/factsheets/ukraine-nuclear-weapons · https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025
- **Question 17 (question)** — ~~a free-fall gravity bomb that takes up to 60 hours to authorize and requires flying into Russian air defenses?~~ → a gravity bomb that needs sign-off from NATO, the US President, and a host government and must be flown into Russian air defenses—when RAND found Russia could reach the Baltic capitals in 36 to 60 hours?
  - Why: No source gives a '60 hours to authorize' figure; the 36–60 hours is RAND's 2016 Baltic wargame time for Russian forces. Release does need an NPG decision plus US President and host-nation approval. Canonical SWEE-2.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2016/04/outnumbered-outranged-and-outgunned-how-russia-defeats.html · https://polskieradio.pl/395/7784/Artykul/3710876,us-open-to-poland-joining-natos-nuclear-sharing-program-expert-says · https://www.defenseone.com/ideas/2020/10/us-should-remove-its-nukes-europe/168876/
- **Question 18 (question)** — ~~When Vladimir Putin deployed tactical nuclear weapons to Belarus in 2023, didn't he explicitly cite NATO's B61 nuclear sharing as his justification?~~ → When Vladimir Putin announced tactical nuclear weapons for Belarus in March 2023, didn't he point to US nuclear bombs in NATO Europe as his precedent, saying 'the United States has been doing this for decades'?
  - Why: On 25 Mar 2023 Putin announced the plan. He cited US weapons in allied countries as precedent ('The United States has been doing this for decades'), but named the UK's depleted-uranium shells as the trigger. Deployment came later. Canonical WIL-2; quote confirmed in AP/NPR reports.
  - Source: https://www.mprnews.org/story/2023/03/25/npr-putin-russia-tactical-nuclear-weapons-belarus · https://www.aljazeera.com/news/2023/3/28/why-does-russia-want-tactical-nuclear-weapons-in-belarus
- **Question 19 (question)** — ~~for banning nuclear weapons on their soil in peacetime?~~ → for refusing nuclear weapons on their soil in peacetime as a matter of policy?
  - Why: Norway and Denmark have peacetime political policies, not laws, against nuclear weapons on their soil (Defense News, Mar 2026). Canonical NORWAY-DENMARK.
  - Source: https://www.defensenews.com/global/europe/2026/03/09/finland-readies-itself-to-be-able-to-host-nukes-in-longtime-policy-departure/
- **Question 22 (question)** — ~~If standard EU sanctions are enough to stop Rosatom, why did EU states still pay Rosatom 781 million euros in 2025 after four years of war?~~ → If EU sanctions can handle Rosatom, why is Rosatom still largely unsanctioned after four years of war, with EU states importing about 781 million euros of Russian nuclear fuel and uranium in 2025?
  - Why: RUSI says Russia's nuclear sector 'remains largely unsanctioned'. The €781M is EU imports of Russian enriched uranium, fuel assemblies and components (Eurostat via RUSI). Canonical RUSI-SANCTIONS-LINGEN fix.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such · https://uatv.ua/en/the-peaceful-atom-that-pays-for-war-why-rosatom-still-escapes-eu-sanctions/
- **Question 23 (trigger)** — ~~They say 301 banks still fund nukes.~~ → They say 301 financial institutions still fund nuclear weapons producers.
  - Why: The 301 are banks, pension funds, insurers and asset managers that finance 25 nuclear weapons producers (DBOTB 2026), not just banks. Canonical DBOTB-301.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/investing_in_the_arms_race
- **Question 23 (question)** — ~~Doesn't the fact that 301 banks backslid on voluntary ESG pledges prove we need binding domestic law under the TPNW to force divestment?~~ → Doesn't a 15 percent jump in nuclear weapons financiers—from 260 to 301—as investors loosen voluntary pledges, prove we need binding law instead of voluntary ESG?
  - Why: 301 is the total number of financiers, up from 260 (about +15%), not 301 institutions that backslid. The TPNW binds states; whether banks are covered depends on national law. Canonical DBOTB-301 and ART1E-FINANCING.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/investing_in_the_arms_race · https://www.malaymail.com/news/world/2026/04/25/study-investment-in-nuclear-weapons-production-rises-as-arms-race-fears-grow/217517 · https://banmonitor.org/positive-obligations-1/the-obligation-to-adopt-national-implementation-measures
- **Question 24 (question)** — ~~If European banks ban nuclear warhead financing under the TPNW~~ → If European banks stop financing nuclear weapons producers under TPNW pressure
  - Why: BAE's and Leonardo's nuclear work is delivery systems (submarines, missiles), not warheads. The TPNW does not itself ban bank financing; that is an interpretation of 1(1)(e) and depends on national law. Canonical ART1E-FINANCING and DBOTB-25.
  - Source: https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/nwproducers/ · https://banmonitor.org/tpnw-prohibitions/the-prohibition-on-assisting-encouraging-or-inducing-prohibited-activities
- **Question 26 (question)** — ~~how is a three-to-one advantage not enough?~~ → how is a nearly three-to-one advantage not enough?
  - Why: 559/190 = 2.94, so 'nearly three-to-one', matching SIPRI-1.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025
- **Question 27 (question)** — ~~disarm overnight without a negotiated, internationally verified timeline?~~ → destroy their warheads overnight instead of under a negotiated, internationally verified plan with a ten-year deadline?
  - Why: Article 4(2) requires immediate removal from operational status, so 'disarm overnight' is partly true. Destruction happens under a verified plan with a 10-year deadline, extendable by up to 5 (1st MSP 2022). Canonical ART4.
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf · https://www.armscontrol.org/act/2022-09/features/first-tpnw-meeting-and-future-nuclear-ban-treaty
- **Question 31 (question)** — ~~When a leader has less than 10 minutes to verify~~ → When a leader may have only minutes to verify
  - Why: The premise rested on the unsourced '10-minute' window. Softened so Con can't attack the number.
  - Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_rocket_incident
- **Question 32 (question)** — ~~When Finland and Sweden joined NATO, didn't both countries explicitly refuse to host nuclear weapons on their territory in peacetime?~~ → Even after Finland's parliament lifted its legal ban on hosting nuclear weapons in June 2026, hasn't Finland's president said there are no plans to station them there in peacetime—and doesn't Sweden still refuse them in peacetime?
  - Why: Neither country joined with a nuclear opt-out. On 17 June 2026 Finland's parliament voted 125–61 to repeal its ban, though President Stubb says there are no plans for peacetime stationing. Sweden keeps a peacetime no-nukes doctrine (Kristersson, 2026). Canonical NORDIC-BALTIC-2026, adapted to fit this trap's trigger.
  - Source: https://euromaidanpress.com/2026/06/18/finland-ends-nuclear-weapons-ban/ · https://www.eduskunta.fi/en/press-releases/parliament-approved-amendments-to-the-nuclear-energy-act-and-the-criminal-code · https://www.aa.com.tr/en/europe/finnish-president-rules-out-peacetime-nuclear-weapons-as-law-debate-grows/3863645
- **Question 35 (question)** — ~~store 150 barrels of gasoline~~ → store about 100 barrels of gasoline
  - Why: The analogy mirrors the B61 count in Europe, which FAS now estimates at about 100, not 150 (KRIS-1).
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **Question 34 (question)** — ~~Why should NATO keep 20 to 50 American nuclear gravity bombs~~ → Why should NATO keep 20 to 30 American nuclear gravity bombs
  - Why: About 50 was the 2019 FAS estimate; current FAS/Bulletin per-base estimates for Incirlik are 20 to 30 (Kristensen, 26).
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2025-12/the-changing-nuclear-landscape-in-europe/ · https://fas.org/publication/nukes-out-of-turkey/
- **Question 34 (question)** — ~~just miles from volatile Middle Eastern conflict zones?~~ → a short drive from the Syrian border and volatile Middle Eastern conflict zones?
  - Why: Incirlik is roughly 60–100+ miles from the Syrian border, not 'just miles'. (Türkiye's 2019 removal from the F-35 program over the S-400 is correct.)
  - Source: https://fas.org/publication/nukes-out-of-turkey/

### PRO — Summary & Final Focus

- **Snippet 2 (label)** — ~~8.3-to-1 Conventional Edge~~ → 8.3-to-1 Spending Edge
  - Why: SIPRI measures total military spending, not conventional spending (SIPRI-2).
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025
- **Snippet 3 (label)** — ~~$4T Divest~~ → $3.9T TPNW Exclusions
  - Why: The real figure is $3.9T held by institutions naming the TPNW (PAX/ICAN 2022), not $4T in divestment. Canonical closing-label fix.
  - Source: https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/RejectingRisk-web.pdf
- **Snippet 1 (text)** — ~~Lewis, 23 at Chatham House proves deterrence relies on sheer luck, as shown by the 1983 Petrov false alarm and the 2009 submarine collision, while Sweeney, 20 proves Europe's 150 B61 bombs take up to 60 hours to authori…~~ → Lewis, 14 at Chatham House finds the world 'has, indeed, been lucky,' from the 1983 Petrov false alarm to the 2009 submarine collision, while Sweeney, 20 shows Europe's roughly 100 B61 bombs (Kristensen, 26) can't reliably get through Russian air defenses, an…
  - Why: Lewis is 2014 and says 'lucky', not 'sheer luck'. The count is about 100, not 150. The '60 hours' is unsourced. The RAND 'Ramstein and Aviano' strike claim was not found in any source, and Ramstein has held no US nukes since about 2005. Canonical LEW-1/3, KRIS-1 and SWEE-2/3.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-03/united-states-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **Snippet 2 (text)** — ~~Van Hooft, 25 confirms advanced conventional deep-strike deters Russia without risking human extinction.~~ → Van Hooft, 25 at RAND Europe shows advanced conventional deep-strike can raise the costs of Russian aggression from the first hours of a conflict without nuclear threats.
  - Why: Van Hooft points to conventional denial capabilities as a way to raise an adversary's costs early in a conflict. He does not say they deter Russia on their own or mention extinction. Canonical VH-2.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html
- **Snippet 3 (text)** — ~~Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI proves European Union states paid 781 million euros in 2025 to Russia's Rosatom, where 38 percent of employees build Russian warheads, and Muñoz, 26 shows 109 financial institutions managing over 4 …~~ → Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI shows European Union states paid about 781 million euros in 2025 for Russian uranium and nuclear fuel from Rosatom, whose weapons complex employed about 38 percent of its staff per SIPRI data, and PAX and ICAN, 23 profile 109 financial in…
  - Why: The 38% is SIPRI's 2016 share of staff at weapons-complex enterprises, not warhead builders. The 109 come from PAX/ICAN 2023, not Muñoz 2026, and that report gives no $4T total (the separate $3.9T is from PAX/ICAN 2022). Canonical RUSI-38PCT and DBOTB-109-4T.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such · https://banmonitor.org/news/is-kazakhstan-assisting-russias-nuclear-weapons-programme · https://www.icanw.org/109_investors_rejecting_nuclear_weapons_dont_bank_on_the_bomb_policies_report
- **Snippet 3 (text)** — ~~PAX and ICAN, 23 profile~~ → PAX/ICAN, 23 profile
  - Why: one spelling of the same citation tag across the app

### CON — Rebuttal cards

- **1. 1983 Petrov / Near Misses & B61 Target (citation)** — ~~Kristensen, 22 (FAS) & Williams, 24 (CSIS)~~ → Kristensen, 26 (FAS)
  - Why: The Kristensen, 22 claim was removed. The Russian warhead figures come from FAS's 2026 Nuclear Notebook (canonical WIL-1).
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://fas.org/initiative/status-world-nuclear-forces/
- **2. NATO Conventional Edge (8-to-1 Spending) (trigger)** — ~~NATO outspends Russia conventionally 8-to-1~~ → NATO outspends Russia more than 8-to-1
  - Why: SIPRI reports total military spending, nuclear included, not 'conventional' spending (canonical SIPRI-2).
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025
- **2. NATO Conventional Edge (8-to-1 Spending) (citation)** — ~~Jones, 25 (CSIS) & Williams, 24 (CSIS)~~ → Jones, 25 (CSIS) & Kristensen, 26 (FAS)
  - Why: The tactical-warhead figure is FAS's (canonical WIL-1).
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **3. Financial Divestment ($4T) & Contractors (chipLabel)** — ~~Financial Divestment ($4T) & Contractors~~ → Bank Divestment & Contractors
  - Why: "$4T" mixes two different reports
- **3. Financial Divestment ($4T) & Contractors (trigger)** — ~~$4 trillion in bank divestment~~ → $3.9 trillion in investment exclusions
  - Why: The trillion figure is PAX/ICAN's 2022 count: institutions holding $3.9T named the TPNW as a reason to exclude nuclear weapons producers (canonical DBOTB-109-4T).
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/109_investors_rejecting_nuclear_weapons_dont_bank_on_the_bomb_policies_report · https://www.icanw.org/press_release_2022_report_101_financial_institutions_restrict_nuclear_weapons_investments
- **3. Financial Divestment ($4T) & Contractors (citation)** — ~~& SIPRI, 26~~ → & SIPRI Yearbook, 26
  - Why: The citation should match the response; the SIPRI claim is from the 2026 Yearbook.
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/media/press-release/2026/increasing-focus-nuclear-weapons-amid-heightened-escalation-risks-new-sipri-yearbook-out-now
- **5. NATO Compatibility / Norway & Denmark (citation)** — ~~North Atlantic Council, 20 & Hill, 21 (Chatham House)~~ → North Atlantic Council, 20 & Harvard Law Clinic, 18
  - Why: Re-attributed to match the corrected response (canonical ART1E-UMBRELLA-NPG).
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons
- **6. B61 Bombs Useless / 60-Hour Release (chipLabel)** — ~~60-Hour Release~~ → Slow Release
  - Why: no source for 24–60 hours
- **6. B61 Bombs Useless / 60-Hour Release (trigger)** — ~~take 60 hours to authorize~~ → need NATO, US-President, and host-government sign-off
  - Why: No source gives a 60-hour B61 authorization time; that number comes from RAND's 36-to-60-hour Baltic overrun estimate (canonical SWEE-2). Use requires an NPG decision plus approval from the US President and the host nation.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2016/04/outnumbered-outranged-and-outgunned-how-russia-defeats.html

- **FL 3C: TPNW Weakens NPT Verification (No Additional Protocol) (final focus line)** — ~~Extend our Hill, 21 NPT verification warrant: TPNW Article 3 fails to require the IAEA Additional Protocol globally and Article 4 delegates warhead elimination to a non-existent 'competent international authority,' erod…~~ → Extend our Hill, 21 verification warrant: Hill confirms attempts to require the IAEA Additional Protocol in the TPNW failed, and Article 4 leaves warhead elimination to a 'competent international authority' still not designated—a weaker rival treaty that unde…
  - Why: Hill confirms only that attempts to require the AP failed; he says the TPNW adopts the NPT's safeguards standard (the NPT doesn't require the AP either), so 'eroding the NPT' must be the debater's claim, not Hill's. The authority isn't 'non-existent'; it hasn't been designated yet. Matches the corrected FL 3C.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/2021-01/2021-01-29-nato-tpnw-hill.pdf

SIPRI's $1.58T is total military spending, nuclear incl…

- **CD 3: Divestment Hits Dual-Use NATO Defense Firms (response)** — ~~yet Muñoz, 26 confirms the 109 TPNW-aligned institutions enforce comprehensive corporate bans cutting off all loans and bonds to the 25 dual-use firms—like BAE Systems, Leonardo, and Airbus—that build Europe's conventio…~~ → yet PAX/ICAN, 23 found 55 of the 109 excluding institutions enforce comprehensive bans cutting off all financing to nuclear weapons producers—including dual-use firms on Muñoz, 26's list of 25, like BAE Systems, Leonardo, and Airbus, that build Europe's conve…

- **CD 4: Political Will Needed for TPNW Can Sanction Rosatom (response)** — ~~They claim political waivers like Lingen prove~~ → They claim the Lingen approval proves
  - Why: There are no EU Rosatom sanctions, so there are no 'waivers'. Lingen was a state-level approval of Framatome/ANF fuel production under a TVEL licence.

  - Why: An explicit RUSI recommendation of direct EU sanctions couldn't be confirmed, so the line uses RUSI's actual argument (treat Rosatom as a military supplier). The TPNW requires destroying warheads, not submarines.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such
- **CD 5: TPNW Art. 1(1)(e) Bans Umbrella & NPG (chipLabel)** — ~~CD 5: TPNW Art. 1(1)(e) Bans Umbrella & NPG~~ → CD 5: TPNW Art. 1(1)(e) Bars the Nuclear Umbrella

  - Why: Norway's and Denmark's no-peacetime-stationing positions are political policies, not legal bans (Defense News, Mar 2026).

- **CD 5: TPNW Art. 1(1)(e) Bans Umbrella & NPG (response)** — ~~Norway and Denmark's peacetime bans, yet Hill, 21 proves Norway and Denmark still legally rely on NATO's nuclear umbrella and sit in the Nuclear Planning Group while France keeps 290 sovereign warheads! TPNW Articles 1 …~~ → Norway and Denmark's peacetime policies, yet Norway and Denmark still rely on NATO's nuclear umbrella and sit in the Nuclear Planning Group—every ally but France does—while France keeps 290 sovereign warheads! Harvard Law Clinic, 18 concludes a TPNW state can…
  - Why: Norway/Denmark have peacetime policies, not bans. Their NPG membership and umbrella reliance are NATO facts (all allies except France sit in the NPG), not confirmed in Hill. The NAC 2020 statement isn't the Strategic Concept; its real line is 'as long as nuclear weapons exist…
  - Source: https://www.nato.int/en/what-we-do/deterrence-and-defence/natos-nuclear-deterrence-policy-and-forces · https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons · https://www.globalsecurity.org/military/library/news/2020/12/mil-201215-nato01.htm

- **CD 5: TPNW Art. 1(1)(e) Bans Umbrella & NPG (final focus line)** — ~~Hill, 21 proves Norway and Denmark still rely on NATO's nuclear umbrella and sit in the NPG, whereas TPNW Article 1(1)(e) makes allied nuclear deterrence completely illegal.~~ → Norway and Denmark still rely on NATO's nuclear umbrella and sit in the NPG, whereas Harvard Law Clinic, 18 concludes a TPNW state can no longer shelter under any nuclear umbrella.
  - Why: The Norway/Denmark points are NATO facts, not confirmed in Hill. 'Makes allied nuclear deterrence completely illegal' overstates Art. 1(1)(e); the citable finding is the Harvard clinic's umbrella conclusion.

  - Why: 515 is all UK+French warheads, and about 50 French ones are air-launched (ASMPA).
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html · https://www.nti.org/analysis/articles/france-nuclear-disarmament/

  - Why: 515 is all UK+French warheads, and about 50 French ones are air-launched rather than submarine-launched.

- **CD 7: Anglo-French Arsenals Create Existential Uncertainty (trigger)** — ~~by citing Chevreuil, 25 that UK/French nukes only protect national survival:~~ → by citing Horschig, 25 that UK/French nukes cannot replace the US umbrella:

- **CD 7: Anglo-French Arsenals Create Existential Uncertainty (response)** — ~~They cite Chevreuil, 25 that Anglo-French arsenals only protect national survival, yet the North Atlantic Council, 20 and van Hooft, 25 at RAND confirm Britain's 225 NATO-assigned warheads and France's 290 warheads crea…~~ → They cite Horschig, 25 that Anglo-French arsenals cannot replace the US umbrella, yet NATO Strategic Concept, 22 says their independent forces 'contribute significantly to the overall security of the Alliance' and complicate adversaries' calculations—Britain'…
  - Why: Chevreuil → Horschig (lead author). Her piece doesn't say 'only national survival'. The 'contribute significantly' and 'complicating the calculations of potential adversaries' lines are from NATO's 2022 Strategic Concept (para 29), not the 2020 NAC statement. Van Hooft supplies the 225/290 numbers but actually doubts UK/French credibility in limited scenarios, so he can't 'confirm' the deterrence…
  - Source: https://pugwash.org/2022/11/08/british-pugwash-note-on-the-absence-of-sole-purpose-in-natos-2022-strategic-concept/ · https://www.nti.org/atomic-pulse/natos-new-strategic-concept-what-it-is-and-why-it-matters/ · https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html
- **CD 7: Anglo-French Arsenals Create Existential Uncertainty (citation)** — ~~North Atlantic Council, 20 & van Hooft, 25 (RAND Europe)~~ → NATO Strategic Concept, 22 & van Hooft, 25 (RAND Europe)
  - Why: The UK/French contribution language is from NATO's 2022 Strategic Concept, not the 2020 NAC statement.
  - Source: https://pugwash.org/2022/11/08/british-pugwash-note-on-the-absence-of-sole-purpose-in-natos-2022-strategic-concept/ · https://www.nti.org/atomic-pulse/natos-new-strategic-concept-what-it-is-and-why-it-matters/
- **CD 7: Anglo-French Arsenals Create Existential Uncertainty (final focus line)** — ~~(North Atlantic Council, 20; van Hooft, 25)~~ → (NATO Strategic Concept, 22; van Hooft, 25)
  - Why: NATO's 2022 Strategic Concept (para 29), not the NAC 2020 statement, says UK/French forces contribute to Alliance deterrence by complicating adversaries' calculations.
  - Source: https://pugwash.org/2022/11/08/british-pugwash-note-on-the-absence-of-sole-purpose-in-natos-2022-strategic-concept/ · https://www.nti.org/atomic-pulse/natos-new-strategic-concept-what-it-is-and-why-it-matters/
- **CD 8: TPNW Art. 4 Authority Doesn't Exist (chipLabel)** — ~~CD 8: TPNW Art. 4 Authority Doesn't Exist~~ → CD 8: TPNW Art. 4 Authority Still Not Designated
  - Why: Article 4's competent international authority hasn't been designated yet (states parties left it open), which is different from saying it can't exist.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/2021-01/2021-01-29-nato-tpnw-hill.pdf
- **CD 8: TPNW Art. 4 Authority Doesn't Exist (response)** — ~~yet Hill, 21 at Chatham House proves asking Britain and France to disarm under an undefined 'competent international authority' that does not exist while Russia and China refuse to sign violates multilateral arms contro…~~ → yet Hill, 21 at Chatham House confirms Article 4 leaves elimination to a 'competent international authority' that still has not been designated—and asking Britain and France to disarm under it while Russia and China refuse to sign abandons multilateral arms c…
  - Why: No Hill passage saying this 'violates multilateral arms control' was found, so that is now the debater's warrant. The authority is undesignated, not non-existent, matching the corrected rebuttal 8.

- **CD 8: TPNW Art. 4 Authority Doesn't Exist (final focus line)** — ~~that does not exist while Russia and China refuse to sign.~~ → that still has not been designated while Russia and China refuse to sign.
  - Why: The authority hasn't been designated; 'does not exist' invites the Pro answer that it will be designated once a nuclear state joins.

- **CD 9: Unilateral Disarmament Invites Unopposed Strikes (response)** — ~~yet Williams, 24 at CSIS shows Russia already targets major NATO airbases like Ramstein and Aviano regardless—and only NATO's nuclear deterrent stops Moscow from striking those bases~~ → yet major NATO airbases like Ramstein—which holds no US nuclear bombs at all—and Aviano are prime Russian targets regardless, and with Russia keeping about 4,400 warheads (Kristensen, 26), only NATO's nuclear deterrent stops Moscow from striking those bases
  - Why: No Williams/CSIS source on Russia targeting Ramstein and Aviano was found, so that is now the debater's warrant. The warhead count comes from Kristensen, 26, matching the corrected rebuttal 9.

- **CD 9: Unilateral Disarmament Invites Unopposed Strikes (citation)** — ~~Williams, 24 (CSIS)~~ → Kristensen, 26 (FAS)
  - Why: Williams, 24 doesn't contain the targeting claim. The only sourced fact is the FAS 2026 warhead count.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://fas.org/initiative/status-world-nuclear-forces/
- **CD 9: Unilateral Disarmament Invites Unopposed Strikes (final focus line)** — ~~Williams, 24 shows Russia targets NATO bases regardless, and only NATO's nuclear deterrent stops Moscow~~ → NATO bases are Russian targets regardless of B61s, and with Russia keeping about 4,400 warheads (Kristensen, 26), only NATO's nuclear deterrent stops Moscow
  - Why: The targeting claim isn't in Williams, 24, so it stays as the debater's warrant. The warhead count comes from Kristensen, 26.

- **CD 10: Putin Keeps Warheads in Belarus Regardless (response)** — ~~Williams, 24 proves Russia built permanent nuclear storage vaults in Belarus for coercion and will keep those warheads on the Polish and Lithuanian border unopposed.~~ → Putin announced Russia would finish a special nuclear storage facility in Belarus by July 2023 (AP, 23)—after Belarus scrapped its non-nuclear status in February 2022 (AP, 22)—and will keep any warheads there next door to Poland and Lithuania unopposed.
  - Why: No Williams/CSIS source for 'permanent vaults' was found. Putin announced on 25 Mar 2023 that a special storage facility in Belarus would be finished by 1 July 2023 (AP), and Lukashenko's 27 Feb 2022 referendum dropped Belarus's non-nuclear status. FAS-identified site work is at Asipovichy in central Belarus, not on the border, and FAS hasn't visually confirmed warheads there.
  - Source: https://abc17news.com/news/ap-national-news/2023/03/25/putin-says-russia-will-station-tactical-nukes-in-belarus/ · https://www.mprnews.org/story/2023/03/25/npr-putin-russia-tactical-nuclear-weapons-belarus · https://www.opb.org/article/2022/02/28/belarus-sheds-neutral-status-in-vote-critics-call-rigged/
- **CD 10: Putin Keeps Warheads in Belarus Regardless (citation)** — ~~Williams, 24 (CSIS)~~ → AP, 23 & AP, 22
  - Why: The storage-facility and referendum facts are AP reporting (Mar 2023, Feb 2022), not Williams, 24.
  - Source: https://abc17news.com/news/ap-national-news/2023/03/25/putin-says-russia-will-station-tactical-nukes-in-belarus/ · https://www.mprnews.org/story/2023/03/25/npr-putin-russia-tactical-nuclear-weapons-belarus · https://www.opb.org/article/2022/02/28/belarus-sheds-neutral-status-in-vote-critics-call-rigged/
- **CD 10: Putin Keeps Warheads in Belarus Regardless (final focus line)** — ~~Williams, 24 proves Putin built permanent nuclear vaults in Belarus for coercion~~ → Putin announced a special nuclear storage facility for Russian warheads in Belarus (AP, 23)
  - Why: There's no Williams source for 'permanent vaults'. Putin's own March 2023 announcement of a storage facility is the citable fact.
  - Source: https://abc17news.com/news/ap-national-news/2023/03/25/putin-says-russia-will-station-tactical-nukes-in-belarus/ · https://www.mprnews.org/story/2023/03/25/npr-putin-russia-tactical-nuclear-weapons-belarus
- **CD 11: 77 Years of Zero Invasion of Nuclear NATO (response)** — ~~since 1949 (North Atlantic Council, 20): nuclear-disarmed Ukraine was invaded twice, whereas nuclear-backed NATO has suffered zero territorial invasions in 77 years.~~ → since the treaty was drafted in 1949 (Hill, 21): nuclear-disarmed Ukraine was invaded twice, whereas NATO territory covered by Article 5 has suffered zero invasions in 77 years.
  - Why: The NAC 2020 statement doesn't make the 'since 1949' claim. Hill (2021) notes that nuclear deterrence has been part of the Alliance's strategic landscape since the treaty was drafted. 'Territory covered by Article 5' heads off the 1982 Falklands rebuttal (UK territory outside the treaty area), matching the corrected rebuttal 11.

- **CD 11: 77 Years of Zero Invasion of Nuclear NATO (citation)** — ~~North Atlantic Council, 20 & Jones, 25 (CSIS)~~ → Hill, 21 (Chatham House) & Jones, 25 (CSIS)

- **CD 11: 77 Years of Zero Invasion of Nuclear NATO (final focus line)** — ~~whereas nuclear-backed NATO has suffered zero invasions in 77 years (North Atlantic Council, 20; Jones, 25).~~ → whereas nuclear-backed NATO territory has suffered zero invasions in 77 years—Russia resorts to sabotage instead (Jones, 25).
  - Why: Neither the NAC 2020 statement nor Jones states the zero-invasions record; it's historical fact. Jones documents Russia's shift to sabotage, so the cite is tied to that.

- **CD 12: Multi-Year Industrial Lag Leaves Window of Danger (response)** — ~~Jones, 25 testified in November 2025 that rebuilding European integrated air defense, satellite intelligence, and deep-strike munition factories takes a decade of industrial lead time—opening~~ → Jones, 25 testified in November 2025 that European militaries still lack enough short-range air defense, long-range fires, and long-range precision strike munitions—and those factories take years to build, opening

'Satellite intelligence' and 'a decade of industrial lead time' weren't found in it, so the timeline becomes the debater's warrant.

  - Why: Jones documented the capability shortfalls; 'multi-year industrial lags' wasn't found in his testimony, so it's separated as the debater's warrant.

  - Why: 'Air-gapped' isn't sourced and Kristensen, 22 doesn't make the claim. The documented facts are US custody, PALs, and NPG/US-President/host-government approval, matching the corrected rebuttal 14.

### CON — Crossfire answers

- **1. 100% Certainty / Infallibility (answer)** — ~~while Russia keeps 4,489 warheads on alert, as Williams, 24 documents~~ → while Russia keeps roughly 4,400 nuclear warheads, as Kristensen, 26 at FAS documents
  - Why: 4,489 is the outdated FAS 2023 stockpile estimate (not from the Williams/CSIS brief), and the warheads aren't 'on alert'. FAS 2026 puts it at about 4,400.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://fas.org/initiative/status-world-nuclear-forces/
- **2. Incinerating Civilians / Moral Trap (answer)** — ~~any nuclear attack triggers instant retaliation against the regime, as van Hooft, 25 at RAND explains~~ → any nuclear attack risks devastating retaliation—van Hooft, 25 at RAND notes Britain and France each have enough strategic weapons to cause unacceptable damage to Moscow and St Petersburg
  - Why: Van Hooft doesn't say 'instant retaliation against the regime'. What he does say is that each country has enough strategic weapons to cause unacceptable damage to Moscow and St Petersburg.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html
- **3. 70 Years Stopping Proliferation (chip)** — ~~70 Years Stopping~~ → Decades Stopping
  - Why: NPT is 1968; "decades" avoids an imprecise year count
- **3. 70 Years Stopping Proliferation (topicLabel)** — ~~Spent 70 Years Stopping~~ → Spent Decades Stopping
  - Why: same as above
- **3. 70 Years Stopping Proliferation (answer)** — ~~NATO has spent 70 years stopping proliferation~~ → NATO has spent decades stopping proliferation
  - Why: '70 years' is loose. The NPT dates from 1968, and NATO's nuclear sharing grew up gradually (canonical MISC-3).
- **3. 70 Years Stopping Proliferation (answer)** — ~~As Chevreuil, 25 at CSIS warns, stripping away that allied nuclear umbrella is what triggers a global proliferation cascade.~~ → When US support wavered in March 2025, Poland's prime minister told parliament Poland must pursue 'the most modern capabilities, also related to nuclear weapons' (Tusk, 25)—and South Korea reopened its own nuclear debate (Axios, 25). Stripping away that allie…
  - Why: The Horschig/Chevreuil CSIS piece wasn't found to make this cascade claim. Re-sourced to Tusk's Mar 7, 2025 Sejm speech and Axios (Mar 19, 2025), per canonical CHEV-2.
  - Source: https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2025-03-07/polish-premier-tusk-to-boost-army-to-half-a-million · https://www.intellinews.com/poland-s-tusk-outlines-push-for-nuclear-weapons-plans-to-expand-army-to-500-000-370665 · https://www.axios.com/2025/03/19/nuclear-weapons-france-poland-china
- **5. Proxy Wars / Vietnam & Sabotage (answer)** — ~~As Jones, 25 at CSIS warns, removing NATO's nuclear deterrent removes the ceiling~~ → Jones, 25 at CSIS documents that Russia wages its war on Europe through covert sabotage rather than open invasion—and removing NATO's nuclear deterrent removes the ceiling
  - Why: Jones (CSIS) documents Russia's sabotage campaign but doesn't argue that the nuclear deterrent is the 'ceiling'. Now his finding is cited and the inference is Con's own.
  - Source: https://csis.org/analysis/russias-shadow-war-against-west
- **6. 8-to-1 Conventional Spending (chip)** — ~~8-to-1 Conventional Spending~~ → 8-to-1 Spending Edge

- **7. Rosatom €781M Uranium Payments (answer)** — ~~explicitly states that the European Union can sanction Rosatom uranium imports directly through standard EU sanctions tomorrow without ratifying the TPNW.~~ → argues Europe must treat Rosatom as a military supplier, which means sanctions and decoupling—tools the European Union already has without ratifying the TPNW.
  - Why: RUSI doesn't say this. It argues Europe must treat Rosatom as a military supplier and notes that the nuclear sector remains largely unsanctioned. EU sanctions need unanimity, which Hungary and Slovakia block, so 'tomorrow' is false.
  - Source: https://www.rusi.org/explore-our-research/publications/commentary/rosatom-military-supplier-europe-must-treat-it-such · https://uatv.ua/en/the-peaceful-atom-that-pays-for-war-why-rosatom-still-escapes-eu-sanctions/
- **7. Rosatom €781M Uranium Payments (answer)** — ~~We do not need to destroy Britain and France's nuclear submarines~~ → We do not need to disarm Britain and France's nuclear submarines
  - Why: The TPNW requires destroying nuclear weapons, not the submarines themselves (they could be converted).
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf
- **8. NATO Unity & Gravity Bombs (answer)** — ~~The North Atlantic Council, 20 unanimously declared that NATO is a nuclear alliance and that TPNW ratification is incompatible with allied defense. Furthermore, Hill, 21 at Chatham House proves that TPNW Article 1(1)(e)…~~ → The North Atlantic Council, 20 unanimously declared that NATO will remain a nuclear alliance and that the TPNW is 'at odds with the existing non-proliferation and disarmament architecture'—and NATO's 2021 summit called it 'inconsistent with the Alliance's nuc…
  - Why: The NAC never said 'incompatible with allied defense'; its real words are 'at odds with…' (2020) and 'inconsistent with…' (2021). Hill's wording on 1(1)(e) isn't confirmed. The umbrella point is sourced to the Harvard Law clinic (2018). The Ban Monitor says nuclear-weapon-related military activities would have to stop.
  - Source: https://www.globalsecurity.org/military/library/news/2020/12/mil-201215-nato01.htm · https://nipp.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/09/IS-501.pdf · https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons
- **9. NPT Article VI Mandate (answer)** — ~~NPT Article VI requires multilateral disarmament under strict and effective international control by all nuclear powers together, not unilateral Western disarmament.~~ → NPT Article VI requires good-faith negotiations on nuclear disarmament and on general and complete disarmament under strict and effective international control—a process the nuclear powers negotiate together, not unilateral Western disarmament.
  - Why: Article VI requires good-faith negotiations. It never says 'multilateral' or 'all nuclear powers together', and 'strict and effective international control' attaches to general and complete disarmament.
  - Source: https://www.un.org/en/conf/npt/2015/pdf/text%20of%20the%20treaty.pdf
- **9. NPT Article VI Mandate (answer)** — ~~Hill, 21 at Chatham House proves the TPNW actually weakens the NPT by creating a competing treaty that fails to require the IAEA Additional Protocol and has zero nuclear-armed adversaries signed on.~~ → Hill, 21 at Chatham House explains NATO's concern that the TPNW risks undermining the NPT, and confirms attempts to require the IAEA Additional Protocol in the TPNW failed—while zero nuclear-armed states have signed on.
  - Why: Hill reports NATO's concern that the TPNW undermines the NPT; he doesn't 'prove' it. He also confirms that attempts to require the AP failed. Note that the NPT doesn't require the AP either.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw
- **10. Why Shouldn't Poland Build Nukes? (answer)** — ~~protected by the US nuclear umbrella and British and French extended deterrence in NATO, as Chevreuil, 25 at CSIS explains.~~ → protected by the US nuclear umbrella and British and French nuclear forces in NATO—and when US support wavered in March 2025, Poland's prime minister told parliament Poland must pursue 'the most modern capabilities, also related to nuclear weapons' (Tusk, 25).
  - Why: This isn't found in the Horschig/Chevreuil CSIS piece, so it's re-sourced to Tusk's Mar 2025 Sejm speech. France doesn't formally extend deterrence.

- **11. What Verification Is Missing? (answer)** — ~~Hill, 21 at Chatham House identifies two exact missing mechanisms: first, TPNW Article 3 fails to require the IAEA Additional Protocol—the gold standard for catching undeclared nuclear facilities—requiring only basic 19…~~ → Two mechanisms are missing: first, as Hill, 21 at Chatham House confirms, attempts to require the IAEA Additional Protocol—the gold standard for catching undeclared nuclear facilities—failed, so TPNW Article 3 only mandates basic 1970s INFCIRC/153 safeguards …
  - Why: Hill confirms that attempts to require the AP failed. Article 3 requires states that already have an AP to keep it, so 'only basic' applies just to states without stronger safeguards. The competent authority hasn't been designated, and the treaty doesn't require that until a nuclear-armed state joins. The Article 4 point isn't sourced to Hill.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw · https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf · https://documents.unoda.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/TPNW.MSP_.2022.WP.1-competent-international-authority-Advanced-Unedited.pdf

Meanwhile, Russia has already named the US missile-defense base at Redzikowo, Poland—which stores no B61 bombs—a priority target (Zakha…

  - Source: https://pugwash.org/2022/11/08/british-pugwash-note-on-the-absence-of-sole-purpose-in-natos-2022-strategic-concept/ · https://www.polskieradio.pl/395/7784/Artykul/3449102,russia-labels-us-missile-defense-base-in-poland-a- · https://pap.pl/en/news/russia-issues-warnings-us-missile-base-poland

- **22. Nuclear Winter / Extinction (answer)** — ~~leaves Russia's 4,489 warheads intact~~ → leaves Russia's roughly 4,400 warheads (Kristensen, 26) intact
  - Why: 4,489 is the outdated 2023 figure; FAS 2026 puts it at about 4,400.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **23. Spend Nuclear Money on Tanks (answer)** — ~~Nuclear deterrence costs a tiny fraction of NATO's 1.58 trillion dollar defense budget, and as van Hooft, 25 at RAND explains, Britain and France's 515 submarine warheads provide an irreplaceable strategic backstop that…~~ → Nuclear deterrence costs a small fraction of NATO's 1.58 trillion dollars in military spending (SIPRI, 26), and as van Hooft, 25 at RAND documents, Britain and France hold 515 nuclear warheads—a strategic backstop that no amount of conventional tanks can repl…
  - Why: 1.58T is the combined military spending of all members, not a NATO budget. The US ($69.2bn) and UK ($12.6bn, ICAN) plus France come to roughly 5–6%, so 'small' rather than 'tiny'. 515 is all warheads, and France's ~50 air-launched ones aren't on submarines. 'Irreplaceable' isn't van Hooft's claim. Russia's total is about 4,400 (FAS 2026).
  - Source: https://www.sipri.org/publications/2026/sipri-fact-sheets/trends-world-military-expenditure-2025 · https://www.icanw.org/global_spending_on_nuclear_weapons_increased_19_in_2025_to_reach_its_highest_recorded_level_at_118_8_billion · https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html
- **25. Russian Hybrid Sabotage (answer)** — ~~Jones, 25 at CSIS explains that Russia uses covert grey-zone sabotage precisely because NATO's nuclear and conventional deterrent makes a direct military invasion of NATO territory too costly for Moscow.~~ → Jones, 25 at CSIS documents that Russia wages its war on Europe through covert sabotage rather than open invasion of NATO territory—and the reason is obvious: NATO's combined nuclear and conventional deterrent makes a direct attack too costly.
  - Why: Jones documents the sabotage campaign but doesn't make the causal claim, so it's now presented as Con's inference.
  - Source: https://csis.org/analysis/russias-shadow-war-against-west
- **26. Global South / 90+ Countries (chip)** — ~~90+ Countries~~ → 100 Countries
  - Why: Oct 2026: 75 states parties + 25 signatories = 100

  - Why: same as above
- **26. Global South / 90+ Countries (answer)** — ~~has joined the TPNW (North Atlantic Council, 20)~~ → has joined the TPNW (ICAN, 26)
  - Why: The current TPNW status (no NATO member and no nuclear-armed state has signed) is documented by ICAN; the NAC attribution wasn't found.

- **26. Global South / 90+ Countries (answer)** — ~~while expanding their arsenals~~ → while modernizing and expanding their arsenals
  - Why: SIPRI Yearbook 2026 describes modernization and expansion. FAS shows Russia's stockpile roughly flat (about 4,400).

- **27. Article 4 Gradual Timeline (answer)** — ~~Hill, 21 at Chatham House proves that under TPNW Article 4, a nuclear-armed state must still legally commit to total unilateral elimination of its arsenal on a fixed deadline without~~ → Under TPNW Article 4, a nuclear-armed state must still legally commit to total unilateral elimination of its arsenal on a fixed deadline—ten years, extendable by up to five—without
  - Why: This is accurate to the treaty text but wasn't confirmed in Hill. The 1st MSP (2022) set a 10-year deadline, extendable by up to 5.
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf · https://www.armscontrol.org/act/2022-09/features/first-tpnw-meeting-and-future-nuclear-ban-treaty
- **28. 2009 Submarine Collision (answer)** — ~~there was zero radiation release and zero risk of nuclear detonation because modern warheads cannot detonate without encrypted electronic arming codes.~~ → Britain's First Sea Lord confirmed no one was hurt and nuclear safety was never compromised—zero radiation leaked.
  - Why: British Trident warheads reportedly lack PAL-type locks (BBC Newsnight, 2007), so 'encrypted arming codes' is wrong. The documented facts are very low speed, no injuries, no radiation, and the First Sea Lord's 'no compromise to nuclear safety'.
  - Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Vanguard_and_Le_Triomphant_submarine_collision · https://www.hurriyet.com.tr/gundem/british-french-nuclear-submarines-collide-in-atlantic-officials-11015704
- **29. Mattis: Tactical Nuke Escalation (topicLabel)** — ~~Didn't Mattis Say Any Tactical Nuke Escalates to Strategic War?~~ → Didn't Mattis Say There's No Such Thing as a Tactical Nuke?
  - Why: Mattis's actual words (HASC, 6 Feb 2018) were: 'I don't think there's any such thing as a tactical nuclear weapon. Any nuclear weapon used any time is a strategic game changer.' He did not say any tactical use 'escalates to strategic war'. The label is now faithful to the quote.
  - Source: https://www.military.com/defensetech/2018/02/06/mattis-there-no-such-thing-tactical-nuke.html
- **29. Mattis: Tactical Nuke Escalation (answer)** — ~~his 1,816 tactical nuclear weapons~~ → his roughly 1,800 tactical nuclear warheads (Kristensen, 26)
  - Why: 1,816 is the 2023 figure; FAS 2026 gives about 1,794 non-strategic warheads.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/
- **30. European Public Opinion Polls (answer)** — ~~Chevreuil, 25 at CSIS documents that since Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine, public support for NATO's nuclear deterrence has risen across Europe, and democratically elected governments in all 32 NATO countries—i…~~ → In April 2026, all 32 NATO governments—including new members Finland and Sweden—reaffirmed in the North Atlantic Council that 'as long as nuclear weapons exist, NATO will remain a nuclear alliance' (North Atlantic Council, 26).
  - Why: The CSIS piece wasn't found to contain a public-opinion finding. The all-32 affirmation is real but comes from the April 2026 NAC statement.

US Strategic Arsenal Still Exists (answer)** — ~~Hill, 21 at Chatham House proves that TPNW Article 1(1)(e) makes it illegal for ratifying European states to rely on or assist any nuclear deterrence—meaning~~ → Even Harvard Law's pro-ban human rights clinic concludes that a state joining the TPNW can no longer shelter under a nuclear umbrella (Harvard Law Clinic, 18)—meaning
  - Why: Hill's wording couldn't be confirmed. The Harvard clinic (2018) concludes that a state party must give up reliance on a nuclear umbrella.

- **33. Euratom Already Has IAEA Protocol (answer)** — ~~Hill, 21 at Chatham House explains that the TPNW sets a weaker global treaty standard under Article 3 that allows non-European states to join without the Additional Protocol, eroding the NPT's global verification standa…~~ → Hill, 21 at Chatham House confirms that attempts to require the Additional Protocol in the TPNW failed, so Article 3 lets states without one join with only a basic comprehensive safeguards agreement—the TPNW missed its chance to raise the global verification …
  - Why: The NPT doesn't require the AP either, so the TPNW can't 'erode' an NPT standard. Hill notes that the attempt to require the AP failed and that it's an open question whether the result is a lower or higher standard.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw · https://www.icrc.org/en/download/file/93623/safeguards-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons-briefing-note-icrc_.pdf

  - Why: Lingen is a Framatome/ANF plant making fuel under a Rosatom (TVEL) licence

  - Why: same as above
- **34. Germany Lingen Rosatom Plant (answer)** — ~~Danylyuk, 26 at RUSI explains that Germany can revoke commercial nuclear fuel licenses and enforce EU sanctions against Rosatom through domestic regulatory action tomorrow—without~~ → Lower Saxony approved the Lingen fuel plant under strict security conditions and says it can revisit that approval if new security risks emerge (Bellona, 26)—German regulators can act without
  - Why: RUSI doesn't say this. There are no EU sanctions on Rosatom to enforce, and German officials said nuclear law gave no basis to refuse Lingen. The approval can be revisited if new risks emerge.

TPNW Article 17 Withdrawal Right (answer)** — ~~TPNW Article 17 requires a mandatory 12-month waiting period before withdrawal takes effect and explicitly prohibits withdrawing while a country is involved in an armed conflict! If Russia attacks Europe,
  - Why: Article 17(3) delays withdrawal; it doesn't prohibit it. A state party to an armed conflict when the 12 months expire stays bound until the conflict ends, and a mere threat doesn't trigger this.

### CON — Crossfire questions to ask

- **Question 1 (question)** — ~~while Russia keeps 4,489 warheads~~ → while Russia keeps roughly 4,400 warheads
  - Why: 4,489 is the FAS 2023 estimate; FAS 2026 (Kristensen et al., May 2026) estimates a stockpile of about 4,400.
