- **Question 2 (question)** — ~~Ukraine gave up every nuclear weapon on its soil in 1994~~ → Ukraine agreed in 1994 to give up every Soviet nuclear weapon on its soil
  - Why: In 1994 (Budapest Memorandum, NPT accession) Ukraine agreed to give up the Soviet warheads; transfers ran until June 1996 and Ukraine never had operational control.
  - Source: https://www.armscontrol.org/factsheets/ukraine-nuclear-weapons
- **Question 3 (trigger)** — ~~1983 Petrov accidental launch~~ → 1983 Petrov false alarm
  - Why: No launch occurred in 1983; Soviet early-warning satellites falsely reported US launches.
  - Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Soviet_nuclear_false_alarm_incident
- **Question 3 (question)** — ~~keeps 4,489 warheads on alert~~ → keeps roughly 4,400 warheads
  - Why: FAS 2026 stockpile is about 4,400; only about 1,800 are deployed and tactical warheads are in central storage, so they are not on alert.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://fas.org/initiative/status-world-nuclear-forces/
- **Question 3 (question)** — ~~defective Russian radar systems~~ → defective Russian early-warning systems
  - Why: The 1983 false alarm came from the Oko early-warning satellite system (sunlight on clouds), not radar.
  - Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Soviet_nuclear_false_alarm_incident
- **Question 5 (question)** — ~~pulling trillions from those contractors~~ → pulling money from those contractors
  - Why: Don't Bank on the Bomb 2026 counts about $1 trillion total ($709bn shares/bonds + ~$300bn loans) from 301 institutions worldwide, mostly American; there is no support for 'trillions' pulled by Europe.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/investing_in_the_arms_race
- **Question 6 (question)** — ~~destroy their nuclear submarines~~ → destroy their nuclear warheads
  - Why: TPNW Article 4 requires eliminating nuclear weapons (and converting or eliminating weapons facilities); it does not require destroying submarines.
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf
- **Question 8 (question)** — ~~When Polish leaders have already warned they may seek nuclear weapons if NATO's umbrella is removed~~ → When Poland's prime minister already told parliament in March 2025, as US support wavered, that Poland must pursue the most modern capabilities, also related to nuclear weapons
  - Why: No Polish warning conditioned on 'removing NATO's umbrella' was found. The real fact: Tusk's 7 Mar 2025 Sejm speech, given after the US paused aid to Ukraine (translations vary, so it is paraphrased here).
  - Source: https://www.intellinews.com/poland-s-tusk-outlines-push-for-nuclear-weapons-plans-to-expand-army-to-500-000-370665 · https://www.eunews.it/en/2025/03/10/polands-rearmament-including-nuclear
- **Question 9 (question)** — ~~all 32 NATO governments unanimously declare in the North Atlantic Council statement that NATO is a nuclear alliance and that the TPNW is incompatible with collective defense~~ → all 30 NATO governments at the time unanimously declare in the 2020 North Atlantic Council statement that NATO will remain a nuclear alliance and that the TPNW is at odds with the existing non-proliferation and disarmament architecture
  - Why: NATO had 30 members in Dec 2020. The NAC statement says the TPNW is 'at odds with the existing non-proliferation and disarmament architecture'; no 'incompatible with collective defense' wording exists.
  - Source: https://www.globalsecurity.org/military/library/news/2020/12/mil-201215-nato01.htm · https://www.chathamhouse.org/2021/01/nato-and-treaty-prohibition-nuclear-weapons/03-natos-concerns-about-tpnw
- **Question 10 (question)** — ~~—both of which are illegal under TPNW Article 1?~~ → —an umbrella that even Harvard Law's pro-ban clinic says a TPNW member must give up (Harvard Law Clinic, 18)?
  - Why: Overstated. Article 1 never names NPG membership or umbrellas. Harvard's IHRC (2018) concludes a TPNW party may no longer rely on a nuclear umbrella; NPG illegality is not established.
  - Source: https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons · https://nato.int/cps/en/natohq/topics_50069.htm
- **Question 12 (question)** — ~~destroy 515 invulnerable submarine-launched ballistic missiles~~ → destroy all 515 of their nuclear warheads, most of them on invulnerable missile submarines
  - Why: 515 counts warheads, not missiles. France has about 290 (about 50 are air-launched ASMPA warheads) and the UK about 225.
  - Source: https://www.rand.org/pubs/commentary/2025/03/nuclear-deterrence-can-britain-and-france-take-on-americas.html · https://www.nti.org/analysis/articles/france-nuclear-disarmament/
- **Question 13 (question)** — ~~striking Ramstein and Aviano airbases with zero fear~~ → striking NATO airbases across Europe with zero fear
  - Why: Canon SWEE-3 says to delete 'Ramstein' in Con crossfire[12], and the first pass missed this string.
  - Source: https://shop.rand.org/products/exploring-the-role-nuclear-weapons-could-play-in-deterring-russian-threats-to-the-baltic-states
- **Question 14 (question)** — ~~pull a single Russian nuclear warhead out of Belarus or Kaliningrad~~ → withdraw the nuclear warheads he says he has stationed in Belarus, or a single nuclear-capable Iskander from Kaliningrad
  - Why: Warheads in Belarus are claimed by Putin and Lukashenko but have not been publicly confirmed by Western officials. Warheads in Kaliningrad are unconfirmed; the nuclear-capable Iskander missiles there are confirmed.
  - Source: https://novayagazeta.eu/en/articles/2024/12/10/lukashenko-says-russian-nuclear-weapons-already-deployed-in-belarus-en-news · https://www.egyptindependent.com/us-intel-officials-no-reason-to-doubt-putin-claims-russia-has-moved-nuclear-weapons-to-belarus/
- **Question 15 (question)** — ~~still lack the air defense and munition stockpiles to fight Russia without US deterrence~~ → still lack enough short-range air defense and long-range precision strike munitions—and urge Washington to keep its forces in Europe
  - Why: Jones's 17 Nov 2025 Helsinki Commission testimony cites shortfalls in short-range air defense, long-range fires, heavy ground forces and precision strike munitions, and urges keeping US forces in Europe. No 'without US deterrence' claim was found.
  - Source: https://www.csis.org/analysis/deterrence-natos-eastern-flank · https://www.csce.gov/hearings/deterrence-on-nato-s-eastern-flank
- **Question 17 (trigger)** — ~~They cite 109 banks divesting $4 trillion.~~ → They cite 109 institutions and $3.9 trillion in TPNW exclusions.
  - Why: 109 comes from PAX/ICAN 2023 (institutions, not all banks, restricting investment); the $3.9T is from 2022's 'Rejecting Risk' (institutions naming the TPNW). There was no $4T divestment.
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/109_investors_rejecting_nuclear_weapons_dont_bank_on_the_bomb_policies_report · https://www.dontbankonthebomb.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/RejectingRisk-web.pdf
- **Question 20 (question)** — ~~or did modern safety locks work with 100 percent effectiveness?~~ → or did Britain's First Sea Lord confirm nuclear safety was never compromised?
  - Why: There is no source for safety locks working '100 percent' in 2009; British Trident reportedly has no PAL-type lock. What is documented: a very-low-speed collision, no injuries, no radiation release, and the First Sea Lord said nuclear safety was not compromised.
  - Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Vanguard_and_Le_Triomphant_submarine_collision
- **Question 23 (question)** — ~~and makes it completely illegal to withdraw if you are in an armed conflict?~~ → and keeps you legally bound for as long as you are in an armed conflict?
  - Why: Article 17(3) lets a state give notice during a war; if it is party to an armed conflict when the 12 months expire, it stays bound until the conflict ends. Withdrawal is delayed, not banned.
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf
- **Question 26 (question)** — ~~a 100 percent monopoly over the weapons that control human survival~~ → a 100 percent nuclear monopoly on the European continent
  - Why: The resolution covers only European NATO members; the US, China and other nuclear states would keep their arsenals, so a global monopoly is false.
- **Question 28 (question)** — ~~that the North Atlantic Council officially states contribute directly to NATO's overall security~~ → that NATO's own Strategic Concept says contribute significantly to the overall security of the Alliance
  - Why: Misattributed. The language is NATO's 2022 Strategic Concept para 29: UK and French forces 'contribute significantly to the overall security of the Alliance.'
  - Source: https://www.nato.int/en/what-we-do/deterrence-and-defence/natos-nuclear-deterrence-policy-and-forces
- **Question 29 (question)** — ~~makes it illegal to assist or encourage nuclear deterrence in any way~~ → bans assisting or encouraging anyone to do what the treaty prohibits, including threatening nuclear use
  - Why: Article 1(1)(e) bars assisting, encouraging or inducing 'anyone' to engage in activity prohibited to a State Party (which includes threat of use under 1(1)(d)). It does not mention 'deterrence'.
  - Source: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/assets/treaties/640-TPNW-EN.pdf · https://humanrightsclinic.law.harvard.edu/publications/nuclear-umbrella-arrangements-and-the-treaty-on-the-prohibition-of-nuclear-weapons
- **Question 31 (question)** — ~~How many of the countries that ratified the TPNW share a direct land border with Vladimir Putin's 4,489 nuclear warheads?~~ → How many of the NATO members bordering Vladimir Putin's Russia and its roughly 4,400 nuclear warheads have ratified the TPNW?
  - Why: Kazakhstan borders Russia and has been a TPNW state party since 2019, so the original question's answer is 'at least one'. No NATO member is a party. 4,489 also updated to about 4,400 (FAS 2026).
  - Source: https://www.icanw.org/kazakhstan · https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://fas.org/initiative/status-world-nuclear-forces/
- **Question 32 (question)** — ~~1,816 tactical nukes~~ → about 1,800 tactical nukes
  - Why: FAS 2026 estimates about 1,794 Russian non-strategic warheads; 1,816 is the 2023 figure.
  - Source: https://thebulletin.org/premium/2026-05/russian-nuclear-weapons-2026/ · https://fas.org/initiative/status-world-nuclear-forces/
- **Question 33 (question)** — ~~When Finland and Sweden joined NATO, didn't both governments sign onto NATO's Strategic Concept declaring that NATO is a nuclear alliance?~~ → Didn't Finland and Sweden join, with no opt-outs, an alliance whose 2022 Strategic Concept says that as long as nuclear weapons exist NATO will remain a nuclear alliance?
  - Why: The 2022 Strategic Concept was adopted in June 2022, while Finland and Sweden were only invitees. They joined in 2023 and 2024, accepting NATO policy with no opt-outs; neither 'signed onto' the document as a member.
  - Source: https://www.nti.org/risky-business/natos-new-strategic-concept-what-it-is-and-why-it-matters/
- **Question 35 (question)** — ~~precisely because Washington, London, and Paris warned of catastrophic retaliation from NATO?~~ → precisely because Washington warned Moscow of catastrophic consequences and the G7, including London and Paris, warned of severe consequences?
  - Why: Sullivan (Sept 2022) warned of 'catastrophic consequences' and the G7 (Oct 2022) warned of 'severe consequences'. There was no NATO retaliation threat, and Macron said France's nuclear doctrine would not be engaged by a nuclear strike in Ukraine.
  - Source: https://www.ksl.com/article/news/world/us-warns-of-catastrophic-consequences-if-russia-uses-nuclear-weapons-in-ukraine/50482587 · https://www.gov.uk/government/news/g7-leaders-joint-statement-on-ukraine-11-october-2022 · https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2022-10-12/france-won-t-deploy-nukes-if-russia-uses-them-against-ukraine

  - Why: Jones's 17 Nov 2025 Helsinki Commission testimony says European militaries still lack enough short-range air defense, long-range fires, heavy ground forces and long-range precision strike munitions, and it urges keeping the US posture in Europe. 'Munition stockpiles', 'intelligence', 'multi-year' and 'to fight without US nuclear deterrence' were not found (canonical JON-1). Same wording as v2. Mi…

  - Why: 301 is the total number of financial institutions (banks, pension funds, insurers, asset managers) financing 25 nuclear weapons producers, up from 260 (about +15%), over Jan 2023–Sep 2025 (canonical DBOTB-301). The report does not say TPNW divestment 'only targets' private Western contractors. The 25-company list is its research scope and includes China's state-owned CASC and India's Bharat Dynam…

- **Snippet 4 (text)** — ~~whereas we point to Ukraine disarming in 1994 and getting invaded twice.~~ → whereas we point to Ukraine giving up the Soviet nukes on its soil in 1994 and getting invaded twice.

- **van Hooft, 25 (RAND Europe) (title)** — ~~Defending Europe?~~ → Defending Europe Against Russian Aggression?

- **Muñoz, 26 (ICAN / PAX) (title)** — ~~Don't Bank on the Bomb 2026 Reports: Moving Away from Mass Destruction & Investing in the Arms Race~~ → Don't Bank on the Bomb Reports: Investing in the Arms Race (2026) & Moving Away from Mass Destruction (2023)

- **North Atlantic Council, 20 & Chevreuil, 25 (title)** — ~~Can France and the UK Replace~~ → Can France and the United Kingdom Replace
  - Why: The real title is 'Can France and the United Kingdom Replace the U.S. Nuclear Umbrella?'

- **Lewis, 23 (Chatham House) (short)** — ~~Lewis, 23 (Chatham House)~~ → Lewis, 14 (Chatham House)
  - Why: The report was published on 28 April 2014. The 2023 date is only a web-page update, so the cite has to read 2014.
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.chathamhouse.org/sites/default/files/field/field_document/20140428TooCloseforComfortNuclearUseLewisWilliamsPelopidasAghlani.pdf
- **Lewis, 23 (Chatham House) (takeaway)** — ~~Declassified nuclear near-misses (including the 1983 Petrov false alarm, the 2009 UK/French submarine collision, and 2013 US Air Force misconduct/command-and-control laxity) prove the world has avoided nuclear war only …~~ → 13 cases of near nuclear use (1962–2002), including the 1983 Petrov false alarm, plus recent incidents (the 2009 UK/French submarine collision and 2013 US Air Force misconduct) that raise concern about safety and command-and-control laxity, show the world has…
  - Why: The report says the world "has, indeed, been lucky", not that luck was the "only" reason. It says individuals acted "often in disobedience of protocol and political guidance", not that they disobeyed "launch protocols". Petrov broke reporting protocol and never received a launch order. The report covers 13 cases from 1962 to 2002. The 2009 collision and the 2013 USAF misconduct are recent inciden…
  - Source: https://www.chathamhouse.org/2014/04/too-close-comfort-cases-near-nuclear-use-and-options-policy · https://www.bristol.ac.uk/news/2014/may/nuclear-report.html · https://science20.com/news_articles/there_have_been_13_nuclear_weapon_nearmisses_since_1962-136028
- **Lewis, 23 (Chatham House) (verbatim)** — ~~“Evidence from many declassified documents, testimonies and interviews suggests that the world has, indeed, been lucky, given the number of instances in which nuclear weapons were nearly used inadvertently as a result o…~~ → “Evidence from many declassified documents, testimonies and interviews suggests that the world has, indeed, been lucky, given the number of instances in which nuclear weapons were nearly used inadvertently as a result of miscalculation or error… individual de…
  - Why: Kept the two sentences confirmed word for word.
